### Chinese Toxic Language Mitigation via Sentiment Polarity Consistent Rewrites
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15297v1)
- **Authors**: Xintong Wang, Yixiao Liu, Jingheng Pan, Liang Ding, Longyue Wang, Chris Biemann
- **Abstract**: Detoxifying offensive language while preserving the speaker's original intent is a challenging yet critical goal for improving the quality of online interactions. Although large language models (LLMs) show promise in rewriting toxic content, they often default to overly polite rewrites, distorting the emotional tone and communicative intent. This problem is especially acute in Chinese, where toxicity often arises implicitly through emojis, homophones, or discourse context. We present ToxiRewriteCN, the first Chinese detoxification dataset explicitly designed to preserve sentiment polarity. The dataset comprises 1,556 carefully annotated triplets, each containing a toxic sentence, a sentiment-aligned non-toxic rewrite, and labeled toxic spans. It covers five real-world scenarios: standard expressions, emoji-induced and homophonic toxicity, as well as single-turn and multi-turn dialogues. We evaluate 17 LLMs, including commercial and open-source models with variant architectures, across four dimensions: detoxification accuracy, fluency, content preservation, and sentiment polarity. Results show that while commercial and MoE models perform best overall, all models struggle to balance safety with emotional fidelity in more subtle or context-heavy settings such as emoji, homophone, and dialogue-based inputs. We release ToxiRewriteCN to support future research on controllable, sentiment-aware detoxification for Chinese.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TOXIREWRITECN, a novel Chinese detoxification dataset specifically designed to preserve the sentiment polarity of offensive language while rewriting it to be non-toxic. The dataset comprises 1,556 annotated triplets, each containing a toxic sentence, a sentiment-aligned non-toxic rewrite, and labeled toxic spans. The data covers various scenarios including standard expressions, emoji/homophone-induced toxicity, and single/multi-turn dialogues. The authors benchmark 17 LLMs (both commercial and open-source) on the dataset, evaluating them on detoxification accuracy, fluency, content preservation, and sentiment polarity. The results highlight that while larger models perform better, even the strongest models struggle to maintain emotional fidelity in subtle and context-heavy settings. The dataset is released to support future research on controllable, sentiment-aware detoxification in Chinese.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation and release of TOXIREWRITECN, which is explicitly designed to preserve sentiment polarity during detoxification in Chinese. This is a significant contribution because existing Chinese toxicity datasets primarily focus on detection and lack sentiment-preserving rewrites. Furthermore, the dataset's coverage of diverse toxicity scenarios, including emoji/homophone-induced and dialogue-based toxicity, expands the scope of detoxification research beyond standard sentence-level toxicity. The work tackles the documented tendency of LLMs to overly sanitize text, an issue that's particularly pronounced in lower-resource languages.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in online moderation: balancing safety with freedom of expression and user intent. By focusing on sentiment-aware detoxification, the research aims to improve user satisfaction and trust in online platforms. The comprehensive benchmarking of various LLMs on the new dataset provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses in this domain.  The paper identifies the difficulties of preserving emotional tone in contexts like emoji or homophone based toxicity, and dialogue-based conversations - providing pointers for further research. This research could lead to the development of more effective and user-friendly content moderation systems that better understand and respect the nuances of human communication. The detailed results on specific toxicity scenarios also offer valuable guidance for model development and fine-tuning. This can eventually lead to improved moderation in the Chinese web and perhaps become a model for the other low resource languages.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel and valuable dataset:** The TOXIREWRITECN dataset fills a gap in the Chinese NLP landscape by providing sentiment-aligned toxicity rewrites.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper thoroughly evaluates a wide range of LLMs across multiple dimensions.
*   **Detailed scenario analysis:** The scenario-specific analysis provides valuable insights into the challenges posed by different types of toxicity.
*   **Clear writing and presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the methodology and results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dataset size:** While significant, the dataset size (1,556 triplets) could be considered modest compared to some English toxicity datasets. A larger dataset could potentially lead to more robust model training and evaluation.
*   **Limited emotion annotation granularity:** The paper acknowledges that the annotation pipeline could be improved by including finer-grained emotion annotations to better capture the nuances of human emotion.
*   **Focus on general offensive language:** While the paper filters out hate speech and personal attacks, a more in-depth analysis of different types of offensive language and their impact on sentiment preservation would be beneficial.
*   **The human preference test is quite limited**: It only assessed neutral vs overly-polite, generated by two specific models. More detailed comparisons with other models and possibly different sentiment axes could have improved the human eval.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5**. While the paper has some weaknesses, the novelty and significance of the TOXIREWRITECN dataset and the comprehensive LLM benchmarking outweigh these limitations. The creation of a sentiment-aware detoxification dataset for Chinese is a significant contribution to the field, particularly given the relative lack of resources compared to English. The paper's detailed analysis and insights into the challenges of sentiment preservation in different toxicity scenarios will be valuable for future research and development in this area. The identified LLM weaknesses (over-sanitization, difficulty with nuanced toxicity) open opportunities for future research. While a larger dataset and finer-grained emotion annotations would be desirable, the current dataset provides a solid foundation for further progress. The overall impact on online safety and moderation in the Chinese language justifies the score.

### AgentThink: A Unified Framework for Tool-Augmented Chain-of-Thought Reasoning in Vision-Language Models for Autonomous Driving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15298v1)
- **Authors**: Kangan Qian, Sicong Jiang, Yang Zhong, Ziang Luo, Zilin Huang, Tianze Zhu, Kun Jiang, Mengmeng Yang, Zheng Fu, Jinyu Miao, Yining Shi, He Zhe Lim, Li Liu, Tianbao Zhou, Hongyi Wang, Huang Yu, Yifei Hu, Guang Li, Guang Chen, Hao Ye, Lijun Sun, Diange Yang
- **Abstract**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) show promise for autonomous driving, yet their struggle with hallucinations, inefficient reasoning, and limited real-world validation hinders accurate perception and robust step-by-step reasoning. To overcome this, we introduce \textbf{AgentThink}, a pioneering unified framework that, for the first time, integrates Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning with dynamic, agent-style tool invocation for autonomous driving tasks. AgentThink's core innovations include: \textbf{(i) Structured Data Generation}, by establishing an autonomous driving tool library to automatically construct structured, self-verified reasoning data explicitly incorporating tool usage for diverse driving scenarios; \textbf{(ii) A Two-stage Training Pipeline}, employing Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to equip VLMs with the capability for autonomous tool invocation; and \textbf{(iii) Agent-style Tool-Usage Evaluation}, introducing a novel multi-tool assessment protocol to rigorously evaluate the model's tool invocation and utilization. Experiments on the DriveLMM-o1 benchmark demonstrate AgentThink significantly boosts overall reasoning scores by \textbf{53.91\%} and enhances answer accuracy by \textbf{33.54\%}, while markedly improving reasoning quality and consistency. Furthermore, ablation studies and robust zero-shot/few-shot generalization experiments across various benchmarks underscore its powerful capabilities. These findings highlight a promising trajectory for developing trustworthy and tool-aware autonomous driving models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AgentThink, a novel framework designed to improve the performance of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) in autonomous driving. The framework integrates chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning with dynamic, agent-style tool invocation. AgentThink incorporates three main components: 1) a structured data generation pipeline that creates tool-augmented reasoning data, 2) a two-stage training pipeline using supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) for training VLMs to invoke tools effectively, and 3) an agent-style tool-usage evaluation method for rigorous assessment of tool invocation and utilization. Experiments on the DriveLMM-01 benchmark demonstrate that AgentThink significantly boosts reasoning scores and answer accuracy compared to existing models, highlighting the potential for trustworthy and tool-aware autonomous driving.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of CoT reasoning with agent-style tool invocation specifically tailored for autonomous driving is a significant contribution. Prior works have explored CoT or tool use individually but not in such a unified and dynamic way. The design of a structured data generation pipeline that incorporates explicit tool usage alongside reasoning chains is also novel. Furthermore, the evaluation protocol, focused on tool selection and integration, directly addresses a gap in current VLM evaluation methods for autonomous driving.

*   **Significance:** The paper's demonstrated performance improvements on the DriveLMM-01 benchmark suggests that AgentThink has the potential to address some critical limitations of current VLMs in autonomous driving. The increase in reasoning scores and accuracy are not incremental; they're quite significant (53.91% and 33.54%, respectively). The results presented indicate potential to enhance safety, interpretability, and generalizability in autonomous systems. Ablation studies confirm the value of both the reward structure and the two-stage training process. Furthermore, the zero-shot performance is impressive, suggesting that the training procedure results in a generalizable reasoning procedure that can be applied to unseen datasets. Finally, the qualitative analysis provides examples of AgentThink invoking tools to correct errors and make better decisions, bolstering claims of enhanced performance.

*   **Weaknesses:** The data and implementation is all the paper has going for it and a closer look reveals many reasons to doubt the effectiveness and generality of the approach. First, the training setup uses data derived from GPT-4, this could indicate that the main value proposition is mimicking outputs from a very large, black box neural network in a structured way. Second, the model still has a low accuracy of 71.35% on DriveLMM-01, which may not even be enough for real world driving.

*   **Potential Influence:** Given the increasing reliance on VLMs in autonomous driving research, AgentThink could influence future development by promoting tool-aware reasoning frameworks. Its structured data generation pipeline and evaluation protocol could set standards for training and assessing such models. The success of GRPO in refining tool-use behavior could also encourage more research into reinforcement learning methods in this domain.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign the paper a score of **7**. AgentThink is a significant step forward. Its integration of CoT and dynamic tool invocation is a novel and promising approach for improving VLMs in autonomous driving, as substantiated by experimental results and insights into tool use behaviors. However, the reliance of GPT-4 for generating the training data limits the overall generality of the approach, thus, it is not high-impact enough to warrant a higher score.

### Multiple Weaks Win Single Strong: Large Language Models Ensemble Weak Reinforcement Learning Agents into a Supreme One
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15306v1)
- **Authors**: Yiwen Song, Qianyue Hao, Qingmin Liao, Jian Yuan, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Model ensemble is a useful approach in reinforcement learning (RL) for training effective agents. Despite wide success of RL, training effective agents remains difficult due to the multitude of factors requiring careful tuning, such as algorithm selection, hyperparameter settings, and even random seed choices, all of which can significantly influence an agent's performance. Model ensemble helps overcome this challenge by combining multiple weak agents into a single, more powerful one, enhancing overall performance. However, existing ensemble methods, such as majority voting and Boltzmann addition, are designed as fixed strategies and lack a semantic understanding of specific tasks, limiting their adaptability and effectiveness. To address this, we propose LLM-Ens, a novel approach that enhances RL model ensemble with task-specific semantic understandings driven by large language models (LLMs). Given a task, we first design an LLM to categorize states in this task into distinct 'situations', incorporating high-level descriptions of the task conditions. Then, we statistically analyze the strengths and weaknesses of each individual agent to be used in the ensemble in each situation. During the inference time, LLM-Ens dynamically identifies the changing task situation and switches to the agent that performs best in the current situation, ensuring dynamic model selection in the evolving task condition. Our approach is designed to be compatible with agents trained with different random seeds, hyperparameter settings, and various RL algorithms. Extensive experiments on the Atari benchmark show that LLM-Ens significantly improves the RL model ensemble, surpassing well-known baselines by up to 20.9%. For reproducibility, our code is open-source at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/LLM4RLensemble-F7EE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-Ens, a novel framework that enhances reinforcement learning (RL) model ensembles by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) for task-specific semantic understanding.  Instead of using fixed strategies like majority voting or Boltzmann addition to combine multiple RL agents, LLM-Ens uses an LLM to categorize states into distinct "situations" based on task descriptions. It then statistically analyzes the strengths and weaknesses of each agent in each situation and dynamically selects the best-performing agent during inference based on the identified situation. The framework is designed to be compatible with various RL algorithms and hyperparameter settings.  Experimental results on Atari benchmarks demonstrate that LLM-Ens significantly improves RL model ensemble performance, surpassing established baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to provide contextual understanding and dynamically select RL agents within an ensemble is relatively novel. It addresses a key limitation of traditional RL ensemble methods, which rely on fixed strategies and lack adaptability to changing task dynamics. The integration of LLMs for state categorization and agent selection represents a significant departure from existing approaches.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the robustness and adaptability of RL agents. By leveraging the semantic understanding capabilities of LLMs, LLM-Ens can effectively combine multiple "weak" agents into a single, high-performing agent. This is particularly valuable in complex and dynamic environments where traditional ensemble methods struggle. The demonstrated performance gains on Atari benchmarks suggest that LLM-Ens could be a valuable tool for addressing real-world RL problems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing RL ensemble methods and motivates the need for a more adaptive approach.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** LLM-Ens is presented as a well-structured framework with clear steps for state categorization, agent performance analysis, and dynamic agent selection.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results on Atari benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of LLM-Ens in improving RL model ensemble performance.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation includes comparisons with established baselines, an analysis of the impact of different hyperparameter settings, and an assessment of the framework's compatibility with various RL algorithms.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The availability of open-source code enhances the reproducibility and accessibility of the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The use of LLMs in LLM-Ens introduces a significant computational overhead. The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational cost and scalability of the framework, which limits its applicability in areas with limited computing resources.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The performance of LLM-Ens relies heavily on the capabilities of the underlying LLM. LLMs can be unreliable and output incorrect information, thus affecting performance.
    *   **Limited Task Diversity:** While the paper demonstrates promising results on Atari benchmarks, the diversity of tasks tested is still limited. Further evaluation on more complex and diverse environments would be beneficial.
    *   **State Space Discretization:** Discretizing the state space can introduce approximation error.

*   **Potential Influence:** LLM-Ens has the potential to influence the development of more robust and adaptable RL agents. It could inspire further research into the integration of LLMs with RL and the development of novel ensemble methods that leverage semantic understanding.
*  **Missing Baselines:** The experiments should've also benchmarked simple ensemble methods, such as simple averaging, to better illustrate the superiority of LLM-Ens.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of RL model ensembles. While the computational cost and reliance on LLMs are limitations, the potential for improved robustness and adaptability makes LLM-Ens a promising approach. The clear problem definition, well-defined framework, and strong experimental results further support the value of the research. However, more detailed complexity analysis would strengthen the overall contribution. Overall, the paper provides a valuable advancement in combining multiple models by considering the semantic state description.

### Sonnet: Spectral Operator Neural Network for Multivariable Time Series Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15312v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Shu, Vasileios Lampos
- **Abstract**: Multivariable time series forecasting methods can integrate information from exogenous variables, leading to significant prediction accuracy gains. Transformer architecture has been widely applied in various time series forecasting models due to its ability to capture long-range sequential dependencies. However, a na\"ive application of transformers often struggles to effectively model complex relationships among variables over time. To mitigate against this, we propose a novel architecture, namely the Spectral Operator Neural Network (Sonnet). Sonnet applies learnable wavelet transformations to the input and incorporates spectral analysis using the Koopman operator. Its predictive skill relies on the Multivariable Coherence Attention (MVCA), an operation that leverages spectral coherence to model variable dependencies. Our empirical analysis shows that Sonnet yields the best performance on $34$ out of $47$ forecasting tasks with an average mean absolute error (MAE) reduction of $1.1\%$ against the most competitive baseline (different per task). We further show that MVCA -- when put in place of the na\"ive attention used in various deep learning models -- can remedy its deficiencies, reducing MAE by $10.7\%$ on average in the most challenging forecasting tasks.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper introduces Sonnet, a novel neural network architecture for multivariable time series (MTS) forecasting. Sonnet utilizes a learnable wavelet transform to operate in the spectral domain, incorporating a novel Multivariable Coherence Attention (MVCA) mechanism to model inter-variable dependencies based on spectral coherence. It also employs a learnable Koopman operator for stable temporal evolution. Empirical results demonstrate that Sonnet outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on various MTS forecasting tasks, including weather forecasting, influenza prevalence prediction, and energy price forecasting. The paper also highlights the effectiveness of MVCA as a stand-alone attention mechanism when integrated into existing architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel architecture with several distinct components. The use of a learnable wavelet transform for time-frequency analysis in conjunction with the Koopman operator is an interesting approach, aiming to blend traditional signal processing techniques with deep learning. The MVCA module is perhaps the most innovative contribution, offering a frequency-domain based approach to capturing inter-variable dependencies. While frequency domain methods are used, coherence in attention is novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the improved forecasting accuracy achieved by Sonnet across a variety of real-world MTS tasks. The results show a consistent outperformance of competitive baselines, especially in the challenging tasks of influenza and weather modelling where many exogenous variables are present. The demonstration of MVCA's effectiveness as a stand-alone attention mechanism enhances its broader applicability and impact. The ablation study helps identify the importance of each component of Sonnet, providing valuable insights for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-motivated architecture design: Each component of Sonnet is justified based on existing theory and empirical observations.
    *   Strong empirical validation: The paper thoroughly evaluates Sonnet on a diverse set of MTS tasks, including established benchmarks and new tasks with carefully curated data.
    *   Novel MVCA module: The MVCA module offers a unique approach to modelling inter-variable dependencies, leveraging spectral coherence in the frequency domain.
    *   Ablation Study: Helps to isolate the effect of the different parts of the network and justify the importance of each part.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited theoretical analysis: While the paper provides a good description of the architecture and its components, it lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of its properties and convergence.
    *   ETT Tasks: The model showed inferior performance to other baselines on ETT tasks, which questions the model's consistent usefulness. The authors attribute this to the limited time span and covariate richness of the ETT data sets, but a more robust model should ideally handle these cases, even if with smaller margins of improvement.
    *   Computational Complexity: The authors briefly mention the GPU computational overhead of existing methods but do not address this point in relation to the proposed model. The lack of a thorough comparison to the existing SoTA is somewhat dissappointing.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in MTS forecasting by:
    *   Encouraging the exploration of frequency-domain methods in deep learning models.
    *   Motivating the development of more sophisticated attention mechanisms that can capture inter-variable dependencies effectively.
    *   Providing a strong baseline for evaluating new MTS forecasting architectures.
    *   Bringing visibility on methods that improve prediction accuracy of MTS forecasting in weather related data.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel architecture that achieves significant improvements in MTS forecasting accuracy across a diverse set of real-world tasks. The MVCA module is a particularly innovative contribution with broad applicability. However, the lack of a more profound theoretical analysis, limited performance on one of the tested benchmarks, and the unclear picture on computational costs reduce the paper's overall impact. Overall, the proposed model provides incremental improvement over the existing SOTA. Considering all of these aspects, I assign a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### FaceCrafter: Identity-Conditional Diffusion with Disentangled Control over Facial Pose, Expression, and Emotion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15313v1)
- **Authors**: Kazuaki Mishima, Antoni Bigata Casademunt, Stavros Petridis, Maja Pantic, Kenji Suzuki
- **Abstract**: Human facial images encode a rich spectrum of information, encompassing both stable identity-related traits and mutable attributes such as pose, expression, and emotion. While recent advances in image generation have enabled high-quality identity-conditional face synthesis, precise control over non-identity attributes remains challenging, and disentangling identity from these mutable factors is particularly difficult. To address these limitations, we propose a novel identity-conditional diffusion model that introduces two lightweight control modules designed to independently manipulate facial pose, expression, and emotion without compromising identity preservation. These modules are embedded within the cross-attention layers of the base diffusion model, enabling precise attribute control with minimal parameter overhead. Furthermore, our tailored training strategy, which leverages cross-attention between the identity feature and each non-identity control feature, encourages identity features to remain orthogonal to control signals, enhancing controllability and diversity. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations, along with perceptual user studies, demonstrate that our method surpasses existing approaches in terms of control accuracy over pose, expression, and emotion, while also improving generative diversity under identity-only conditioning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FaceCrafter: Identity-Conditional Diffusion with Disentangled Control over Facial Pose, Expression, and Emotion":

**Summary:**

The paper presents FaceCrafter, a novel identity-conditional diffusion model designed to provide disentangled control over facial pose, expression, and emotion while preserving identity. It achieves this through two lightweight control modules embedded within the cross-attention layers of a base diffusion model (Arc2Face). A tailored training strategy that emphasizes orthogonality between identity features and the control signals further enhances disentanglement and diversity. The authors demonstrate through quantitative and qualitative evaluations, including user studies, that FaceCrafter outperforms existing methods in terms of control accuracy, generative diversity, and identity preservation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the architecture of FaceCrafter and its associated training strategy. While identity-conditional face generation and attribute control are not entirely new, the specific combination of lightweight control modules within cross-attention, coupled with the attention disentanglement loss, represents a significant improvement over previous approaches. The idea of enforcing orthogonality between identity and attribute representations is a valuable contribution to disentangled representation learning in the context of face generation. The incorporation of separate modules for Pose/Expression and Emotion control is also useful, though it is not a groundbreaking departure from existing techniques. The "Intra-Batch Mixed Conditioning" training strategy is a well-designed technique to increase disentanglement.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its ability to produce high-quality, controllable facial images while preserving identity. This has applications in various domains, including realistic avatar creation, synthetic data generation for face recognition tasks, and improved face editing tools. The lightweight nature of the control modules is a positive attribute, making it potentially easier to integrate into existing pipelines compared to methods that require substantial parameter overhead (e.g., ControlNet). The quantitative and qualitative evaluations seem to support the claims made by the authors. The user study is a strong point, as it provides human-centric feedback on the model's performance, which is often overlooked in similar studies. The comparisons against current state-of-the-art is important to demonstrate the significance of their contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined architecture and training strategy.
    *   Disentangled attribute control (pose, expression, emotion).
    *   Strong quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   Incorporation of user studies.
    *   Lightweight control modules.
    *   Clear presentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on Arc2Face as the base model could be seen as a limitation. A direct comparison against other recent diffusion models with similar control capabilities might have been more compelling.
    *   The quantitative metrics, while comprehensive, are subject to biases inherent in the evaluation datasets and pre-trained feature extractors.
    *   The ablation study provides only very limited information regarding the impact of AD loss.
    *   A more thorough analysis of failure cases would strengthen the study.
    *   The method is compared with baselines which use ContolNet, that adds a large amount of parameters. The method is not directly compared to the method IP-Adapter, which inspired it, limiting the analysis of its improvement.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in identity-conditional face generation and controllable image synthesis. The architecture and training strategy could serve as a template for other researchers aiming to achieve fine-grained control over generative models. The focus on disentanglement and diversity is also likely to inspire further work in these areas. The lightweight approach to control is highly useful.

*Score: 7.5*

**Justification:**

FaceCrafter is a solid contribution to the field of controllable face generation. It offers a novel approach to disentangled attribute control with clear results. While it relies on Arc2Face and may not be groundbreaking in every aspect, the specific combination of architectural elements and the training strategy is well-designed and effective. The user study adds significant weight to the claims. The model and architecture are overall very well designed. Overall, FaceCrafter provides a useful architecture to build on, but is limited by it's dependency on Arc2Face. The method deserves a score of 7.5, as it makes meaningful advancement over existing techniques and offers potential for further development.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Emotional Supporters often Use Multiple Strategies in a Single Turn
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15316v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Bai, Guanyi Chen, Tingting He, Chenlian Zhou, Yu Liu
- **Abstract**: Emotional Support Conversations (ESC) are crucial for providing empathy, validation, and actionable guidance to individuals in distress. However, existing definitions of the ESC task oversimplify the structure of supportive responses, typically modelling them as single strategy-utterance pairs. Through a detailed corpus analysis of the ESConv dataset, we identify a common yet previously overlooked phenomenon: emotional supporters often employ multiple strategies consecutively within a single turn. We formally redefine the ESC task to account for this, proposing a revised formulation that requires generating the full sequence of strategy-utterance pairs given a dialogue history. To facilitate this refined task, we introduce several modelling approaches, including supervised deep learning models and large language models. Our experiments show that, under this redefined task, state-of-the-art LLMs outperform both supervised models and human supporters. Notably, contrary to some earlier findings, we observe that LLMs frequently ask questions and provide suggestions, demonstrating more holistic support capabilities.
- **Summary**: The paper identifies a limitation in the standard Emotional Support Conversation (ESC) task formulation. Through a detailed analysis of the ESConv dataset, the authors discover that emotional supporters frequently employ multiple strategies consecutively within a single turn, a phenomenon they term "Consecutive Use of multiple Strategies" (CUS). The paper refines the ESC task to explicitly model CUS, proposing a new task definition, dataset preprocessing steps, and several modeling approaches, including supervised deep learning and LLM-based systems. The experiments demonstrate that under the refined task, LLMs outperform both supervised models and human supporters in corpus-based human evaluation settings, suggesting prior LLM limitations in ESC stemmed from flawed task definitions.

**Novelty and Significance Evaluation:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its identification and formalization of the CUS phenomenon within the ESC task. Prior work largely treated supportive responses as single strategy-utterance pairs, potentially oversimplifying the complexity of real-world emotional support exchanges. The revised task definition and accompanying dataset modifications are a valuable contribution. By explicitly accounting for CUS, the paper provides a more nuanced and realistic framework for building and evaluating ESDSes.

However, the paper's novelty is somewhat tempered by the incremental nature of its modeling approaches. While the supervised models (MCC, MTL, CP) are reasonably well-motivated, they are based on existing BlenderBot architecture, and their modifications are relatively straightforward. The LLM approach, while achieving strong results, relies on standard prompting techniques, albeit with a well-crafted prompt. The paper's significance hinges on whether this revised task formulation will have real-world impact.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear identification and formalization of the CUS phenomenon.
    *   Revised ESC task definition aligned with observed data patterns.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation, including automatic metrics and human evaluation.
    *   Demonstration that LLMs can perform effectively in ESC when provided with a more accurate task setup.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited innovation in the modeling approaches themselves, relying largely on existing architectures and techniques.
    *   The human evaluation setting, while adequate, is limited and lacks the richness of a real-time interactive evaluation.
    *   The paper acknowledges some bias to responses that are longer and more comprehensive - a characteristic of LLM output. This may inflate evaluation results of the larger models (GPT-4 and DeepSeek-R1).
*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The refined ESC task definition and benchmark can guide future research in the field, promoting the development of more realistic and effective ESDSes.
    *   The findings can encourage a re-evaluation of prior work on LLMs for ESC, prompting researchers to revisit existing models and techniques in light of the revised task formulation.

Overall, the paper is a solid contribution to the field of emotional support dialogue systems. The identification of CUS and the subsequent task refinement are valuable contributions, making the ESC task more closely aligned to realistic supportive conversations. However, the modest novelty in modeling and the limited nature of the human evaluation prevent this work from being an exceptional contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving LLM First-Token Predictions in Multiple-Choice Question Answering via Prefilling Attack
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15323v1)
- **Authors**: Silvia Cappelletti, Tobia Poppi, Samuele Poppi, Zheng-Xin Yong, Diego Garcia-Olano, Marcella Cornia, Lorenzo Baraldi, Rita Cucchiara
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly evaluated on multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) tasks using *first-token probability* (FTP), which selects the answer option whose initial token has the highest likelihood. While efficient, FTP can be fragile: models may assign high probability to unrelated tokens (*misalignment*) or use a valid token merely as part of a generic preamble rather than as a clear answer choice (*misinterpretation*), undermining the reliability of symbolic evaluation. We propose a simple solution: the *prefilling attack*, a structured natural-language prefix (e.g., "*The correct option is:*") prepended to the model output. Originally explored in AI safety, we repurpose prefilling to steer the model to respond with a clean, valid option, without modifying its parameters. Empirically, the FTP with prefilling strategy substantially improves accuracy, calibration, and output consistency across a broad set of LLMs and MCQA benchmarks. It outperforms standard FTP and often matches the performance of open-ended generation approaches that require full decoding and external classifiers, while being significantly more efficient. Our findings suggest that prefilling is a simple, robust, and low-cost method to enhance the reliability of FTP-based evaluation in multiple-choice settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates improving the reliability of First-Token Probability (FTP) based evaluation for Multiple-Choice Question Answering (MCQA) using Large Language Models (LLMs). FTP, while efficient, can be fragile due to token misalignment and misinterpretation. The authors propose a "prefilling attack" - prepending a structured natural language prefix to the model's output (e.g., "The correct option is:") to steer the model towards generating a clean, valid answer option. Experiments across various LLMs and MCQA benchmarks demonstrate that this simple prefilling strategy significantly improves accuracy, calibration, and output consistency compared to standard FTP, often matching the performance of more computationally expensive open-ended generation approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Simple and Effective:** The proposed prefilling attack is remarkably simple to implement and doesn't require modifying model parameters or retraining. Its effectiveness across diverse LLMs and datasets highlights its robustness.
*   **Addresses a Real Problem:**  The paper tackles a significant limitation of FTP - its fragility and susceptibility to misalignment and misinterpretation. These are known issues that can lead to inaccurate evaluations of LLMs.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are thorough, covering a wide range of LLMs (including popular open-source models) and diverse MCQA benchmarks. The authors consider various aspects of evaluation, including accuracy, calibration, and output consistency. The comparison with open-ended generation provides valuable context.
*   **Clear Analysis:** The paper provides a clear explanation of the problem, the proposed solution, and the experimental results. The visual examples (Figure 1 & 2) and tables (1, 2, 3, 4, 5) are well-presented and support the claims.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Technique:** The concept of prefilling or prompting to influence model output isn't entirely new, particularly in AI safety. The novelty lies more in its application to the *specific* problem of improving FTP-based MCQA evaluation. The paper acknowledges this connection to AI safety research.
*   **Template Sensitivity:** While the paper argues for robustness across different templates (Appendix A.1), the core experiments rely on a fixed template. Further investigation into adapting the prefilling strategy for different models and tasks could strengthen the approach.
*   **English-Centric:** The study is limited to English language benchmarks. The effectiveness of the prefilling strategy in other languages may vary due to different linguistic structures and tokenization schemes.
*  **No theoretical guarantee**: The paper lacks any theoretical guarantees on why and when the prefilling attack works.

**Significance and Potential Impact:**

The paper has significant practical implications for evaluating LLMs in MCQA settings. The prefilling attack offers a simple and cost-effective way to improve the reliability of FTP-based evaluation, making it a more robust and trustworthy metric. The findings could be valuable for researchers and practitioners who rely on MCQA benchmarks to assess the capabilities of LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

While the technical novelty is somewhat limited, the paper's *application* of prefilling to improve FTP-based MCQA evaluation is novel, impactful, and the implementation is quite practical. The comprehensiveness of the experiments and the clear analysis support the claims and demonstrate the benefits of the proposed strategy. The issues of model performance in languages other than English are important considerations to improve, but do not detract from the clear result. Considering all these points, I believe this paper deserves a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Zero-Shot Differential Morphing Attack Detection with Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15332v1)
- **Authors**: Ria Shekhawat, Hailin Li, Raghavendra Ramachandra, Sushma Venkatesh
- **Abstract**: Leveraging the power of multimodal large language models (LLMs) offers a promising approach to enhancing the accuracy and interpretability of morphing attack detection (MAD), especially in real-world biometric applications. This work introduces the use of LLMs for differential morphing attack detection (D-MAD). To the best of our knowledge, this is the first study to employ multimodal LLMs to D-MAD using real biometric data. To effectively utilize these models, we design Chain-of-Thought (CoT)-based prompts to reduce failure-to-answer rates and enhance the reasoning behind decisions. Our contributions include: (1) the first application of multimodal LLMs for D-MAD using real data subjects, (2) CoT-based prompt engineering to improve response reliability and explainability, (3) comprehensive qualitative and quantitative benchmarking of LLM performance using data from 54 individuals captured in passport enrollment scenarios, and (4) comparative analysis of two multimodal LLMs: ChatGPT-4o and Gemini providing insights into their morphing attack detection accuracy and decision transparency. Experimental results show that ChatGPT-4o outperforms Gemini in detection accuracy, especially against GAN-based morphs, though both models struggle under challenging conditions. While Gemini offers more consistent explanations, ChatGPT-4o is more resilient but prone to a higher failure-to-answer rate.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper explores the use of multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically ChatGPT-40 and Gemini, for differential morphing attack detection (D-MAD). It's framed as the first study to apply LLMs to D-MAD using real biometric data (collected with user consent) rather than synthetic data. The authors design Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompts to improve the reliability and explainability of the LLM decisions. The evaluation involves 54 real subjects in passport enrollment scenarios.  The paper benchmarks the performance of ChatGPT-40 and Gemini, comparing their accuracy and explanation capabilities. Results indicate that ChatGPT-40 is more accurate, especially against GAN-based morphs, but is more prone to failure-to-answer. Gemini offers more consistent explanations, but is less accurate.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a reasonably novel contribution by being, as it claims, the first to apply multimodal LLMs to differential morphing attack detection using real biometric data.  Previous work had explored LLMs in single-image MAD (S-MAD), but this study extends it to the more practical and robust D-MAD.  The CoT prompt engineering for this specific task is also a notable contribution, as is the head-to-head comparison of two prominent LLMs in this context.

*   **Significance:** Morphing attack detection is a critical security concern for automated border control and identity verification systems. Demonstrating that LLMs can contribute, even if imperfectly, is significant. The use of real biometric data, rather than only synthetic data, increases the real-world relevance of the study. Providing explanations for decisions enhances trust and transparency, which is crucial in security applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   First application of multimodal LLMs for D-MAD with real biometric data.
    *   Careful design of CoT prompts to improve reliability and explainability.
    *   Comprehensive quantitative and qualitative evaluation.
    *   Comparison of two prominent LLMs.
    *   Ethical considerations are addressed by using a semi-public dataset, with consent from individuals to use their facial data for this study.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance of the LLMs is still limited, particularly under challenging conditions. This suggests that LLMs are not a "silver bullet" solution and require further refinement and adaptation.
    *   The reliance on specific LLMs (ChatGPT-40 and Gemini) limits the generalizability of the findings. Future work should evaluate a broader range of LLMs.
    *   While the use of real biometric data is a strength, the dataset size (54 subjects) is relatively small. A larger and more diverse dataset would improve the robustness of the findings.
    *   The reliance on specific morphing techniques (LMA, MIPGAN2, PIPE) could limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The "failure-to-answer" issue with ChatGPT is a serious limitation and needs to be addressed.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in this area by demonstrating the potential of LLMs for D-MAD. It also highlights the importance of CoT prompt engineering and explainability. The paper's limitations also provide clear directions for future research, such as exploring other LLMs, using larger and more diverse datasets, and improving the robustness of LLM decisions. However, the limited improvement over existing approaches suggests the influence might be restricted.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper offers a reasonable advancement within a well-defined and current research area. The application of multimodal LLMs to D-MAD with real biometric data is a worthwhile step. The contributions are clearly articulated. However, the limitations discussed, especially in absolute performance and the limited dataset size, prevent it from being considered a truly exceptional contribution. LLMs are now so ubiquitous that the act of applying one to a domain isn't revolutionary on its own.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### My Face Is Mine, Not Yours: Facial Protection Against Diffusion Model Face Swapping
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15336v1)
- **Authors**: Hon Ming Yam, Zhongliang Guo, Chun Pong Lau
- **Abstract**: The proliferation of diffusion-based deepfake technologies poses significant risks for unauthorized and unethical facial image manipulation. While traditional countermeasures have primarily focused on passive detection methods, this paper introduces a novel proactive defense strategy through adversarial attacks that preemptively protect facial images from being exploited by diffusion-based deepfake systems. Existing adversarial protection methods predominantly target conventional generative architectures (GANs, AEs, VAEs) and fail to address the unique challenges presented by diffusion models, which have become the predominant framework for high-quality facial deepfakes. Current diffusion-specific adversarial approaches are limited by their reliance on specific model architectures and weights, rendering them ineffective against the diverse landscape of diffusion-based deepfake implementations. Additionally, they typically employ global perturbation strategies that inadequately address the region-specific nature of facial manipulation in deepfakes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "My Face Is Mine, Not Yours: Facial Protection Against Diffusion Model Face Swapping" introduces a novel proactive defense strategy against diffusion-based deepfake face-swapping. The approach leverages adversarial attacks, specifically designed to disrupt the identity transfer process in diffusion models. The core innovations are: (1) a face identity loss that targets the conditional mechanisms in diffusion-based face swapping and (2) an inference-step averaging technique for efficient generation of robust adversarial perturbations.  By operating in the latent space of diffusion models and averaging deviation losses across multiple inference steps, the method aims to generate effective perturbations while minimizing computational costs and preserving visual fidelity. The paper demonstrates robust protection against various deepfake systems, even when faced with common adversarial defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in developing an adversarial defense mechanism specifically tailored for diffusion-based face-swapping. Existing methods were designed for GANs or employed global perturbations, making them ineffective against the nuances of diffusion models and facial manipulation.  The dual-loss framework, particularly the inference-step averaging technique, addresses key challenges related to gradient instability and memory consumption in diffusion model optimization. While adversarial attacks are not new, their application and adaptation to the specific challenges of diffusion-based face swapping are a significant contribution. The face identity loss is a reasonable and targeted way to disrupt the identity transfer in face-swapping models.

*   **Significance:** The increasing sophistication and accessibility of deepfake technology make the proactive defense strategy presented in this paper highly significant. The paper tackles a real and growing threat to privacy, security, and information integrity. By providing a defense mechanism specifically designed for diffusion models, the paper addresses a critical vulnerability that existing methods fail to address. The results demonstrate the method's effectiveness against a wide range of deepfake systems, including those with different architectures and training methodologies. If the method generalizes beyond the tested systems and datasets (a limitation discussed below), it could significantly impact the development of more secure and privacy-preserving synthetic media technologies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a timely and crucial problem in the field of deepfake technology.
    *   Introduces a novel and effective defense mechanism specifically designed for diffusion-based face swapping.
    *   Proposes a computationally efficient solution by operating in the latent space and employing inference-step averaging.
    *   Demonstrates robustness against a range of deepfake systems and adversarial defenses.
    *   Maintains visual fidelity of protected images.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental validation, while extensive, is limited to specific datasets and diffusion model architectures. The generalizability of the method to unseen datasets, different face swapping architectures, and real-world scenarios needs further investigation. The paper claims that the method maintains effectiveness even against diffusion models using different architectures and training methodologies, but it is not as effective as it might be with more similar architectures.
    *   The defense assumes the availability of access to a pre-trained diffusion model or a proxy of it. Real-world deployment may require the attacker to design an attack without such model access.
    *   The "Purification Resistant Attack" may not be as robust as initially demonstrated if attackers adapt to specifically target SDEdit-augmented perturbations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach could influence the development of more robust and privacy-preserving deepfake detection and prevention technologies. It could also inform the design of new diffusion-based systems that are inherently more resistant to adversarial attacks. The method's computational efficiency could make it practical for real-world applications. It sets a strong foundation for future work in proactively securing facial data against unauthorized manipulation.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of deepfake protection. The targeted adversarial defense mechanism for diffusion-based face swapping is a valuable advancement. However, the limited generalizability and potential for future attacks represent notable weaknesses. While the approach is computationally efficient and preserves visual fidelity, further research is needed to address these limitations and ensure its effectiveness in real-world scenarios. The paper has the potential to influence future research and development of more secure and privacy-preserving synthetic media technologies, but it requires more work.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Your Language Model Can Secretly Write Like Humans: Contrastive Paraphrase Attacks on LLM-Generated Text Detectors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15337v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Fang, Jiawei Kong, Tianqu Zhuang, Yixiang Qiu, Kuofeng Gao, Bin Chen, Shu-Tao Xia, Yaowei Wang, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: The misuse of large language models (LLMs), such as academic plagiarism, has driven the development of detectors to identify LLM-generated texts. To bypass these detectors, paraphrase attacks have emerged to purposely rewrite these texts to evade detection. Despite the success, existing methods require substantial data and computational budgets to train a specialized paraphraser, and their attack efficacy greatly reduces when faced with advanced detection algorithms. To address this, we propose \textbf{Co}ntrastive \textbf{P}araphrase \textbf{A}ttack (CoPA), a training-free method that effectively deceives text detectors using off-the-shelf LLMs. The first step is to carefully craft instructions that encourage LLMs to produce more human-like texts. Nonetheless, we observe that the inherent statistical biases of LLMs can still result in some generated texts carrying certain machine-like attributes that can be captured by detectors. To overcome this, CoPA constructs an auxiliary machine-like word distribution as a contrast to the human-like distribution generated by the LLM. By subtracting the machine-like patterns from the human-like distribution during the decoding process, CoPA is able to produce sentences that are less discernible by text detectors. Our theoretical analysis suggests the superiority of the proposed attack. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of CoPA in fooling text detectors across various scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, along with a novelty/significance score:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Contrastive Paraphrase Attack (CoPA), a training-free method to bypass AI-generated text detectors using off-the-shelf LLMs. CoPA cleverly constructs two types of prompts: one for human-like text generation and another for machine-like text generation.  It then uses the machine-like distribution as a negative contrast to refine the human-like distribution during the decoding process, effectively removing machine-inherent patterns and producing text that is less detectable.  The authors provide theoretical analysis and extensive experimental results showing CoPA's effectiveness against several text detectors and across various datasets. They also explore factors affecting the attack’s performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the training-free and contrastive approach. Previous paraphrase attacks often required training specialized paraphrasers, incurring significant overhead. The idea of using a *machine-like* distribution as a contrastive element to *purify* the *human-like* distribution is innovative and addresses a key weakness of simply prompting LLMs for human-like outputs: their inherent statistical biases. The theoretical justification is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications for the robustness of AI-generated text detection. The demonstration that off-the-shelf LLMs can be used to effectively bypass even sophisticated detectors, without training, is concerning. It highlights the arms race between detection and evasion techniques. This underscores the need for more resilient detection mechanisms and a deeper understanding of LLM biases. The paper contributes to the red-teaming effort and provides a practical and easily implementable attack strategy that researchers can use to evaluate their detectors. It also provides an additional method for better understanding LLM behavior.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free:** Eliminates the data and computational burden of training dedicated paraphrasers.
    *   **Contrastive approach:** Novel use of a machine-like distribution to refine human-like text generation.
    *   **Strong experimental validation:**  Evaluated against multiple detectors and datasets, demonstrating significant performance gains.
    *   **Theoretical justification:** Provides a theoretical framework to support the approach.
    *   **Practical implementation:** CoPA is easy to implement with readily available resources.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational overhead:**  The contrastive mechanism requires two forward passes through the LLM, increasing inference latency, which could be a concern in real-time applications. Although this overhead is smaller than that of training a dedicated paraphraser.
    *   **Limited language scope:** The focus is exclusively on English. While the underlying principles may be transferable, adaptation to other languages requires further research.
    *   **Specific prompt sensitivity:** Although the paper investigates prompts, fine-tuning prompts remains a potential point of failure. Different LLMs might need different crafting.
    *   **Defense remains limited**: The adaptive defense study is preliminary. The defense rate of 78% leaves room for improving detection performance against COPA.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper will likely influence research in AI-generated text detection and red-teaming. The CoPA approach provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating detector robustness. The contrastive method could be adapted for other tasks beyond text evasion. It could also spur research into new detection methods that are less susceptible to paraphrase attacks.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a well-motivated, novel, and rigorously evaluated attack on AI-generated text detectors. The training-free nature and contrastive approach make it a significant contribution. While limitations exist regarding computational overhead, language scope, and more robust adaptive defenses, the paper provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating detector robustness and offers clear insights into manipulating LLM behavior for text evasion. The theoretical analysis and strong experimental results warrant a high score, as the paper significantly advances our understanding of the challenges in detecting AI-generated text.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### SSR: Speculative Parallel Scaling Reasoning in Test-time
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15340v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanlin Chu, Bo Wang, Xiang Liu, Hong Chen, Aiwei Liu, Xuming Hu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have achieved impressive results on multi-step mathematical reasoning, yet at the cost of high computational overhead. This challenge is particularly acute for test-time scaling methods such as parallel decoding, which increase answer diversity but scale poorly in efficiency. To address this efficiency-accuracy trade-off, we propose SSR (Speculative Parallel Scaling Reasoning), a training-free framework that leverages a key insight: by introducing speculative decoding at the step level, we can accelerate reasoning without sacrificing correctness. SSR integrates two components: a Selective Parallel Module (SPM) that identifies a small set of promising reasoning strategies via model-internal scoring, and Step-level Speculative Decoding (SSD), which enables efficient draft-target collaboration for fine-grained reasoning acceleration. Experiments on three mathematical benchmarks-AIME 2024, MATH-500, and LiveMathBench - demonstrate that SSR achieves strong gains over baselines. For instance, on LiveMathBench, SSR improves pass@1 accuracy by 13.84% while reducing computation to 80.5% of the baseline FLOPs. On MATH-500, SSR reduces compute to only 30% with no loss in accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SSR: Speculative Parallel Scaling Reasoning in Test-time":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SSR (Speculative Parallel Scaling Reasoning), a novel, training-free inference framework designed to improve the efficiency and accuracy trade-off in large language models (LLMs) for multi-step mathematical reasoning. SSR integrates two key components: a Selective Parallel Module (SPM) that introspectively selects a small set of promising reasoning strategies from a curated pool, and Step-level Speculative Decoding (SSD) that uses a lightweight draft model to generate candidate reasoning steps, verified and optionally revised by a larger target model. This approach aims to accelerate reasoning by verifying reasoning steps rather than individual tokens and reduce redundant computation. Experiments on AIME 2024, MATH-500, and LiveMathBench demonstrate that SSR achieves better accuracy and efficiency compared to baseline and parallel decoding methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the integration of selective parallel scaling with step-level speculative decoding for multi-step reasoning. While speculative decoding and parallel inference are existing techniques, the combined approach at the *step level* and with selective strategy instantiation is a unique contribution. The strategy pool and the method of creating it seem innovative, too. It builds on previous token-level speculative approaches but expands the verification to semantic coherence over full steps, addressing a key limitation for reasoning tasks. The paper has also innovatively proposed a new method for creating the strategy pool with low effort.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from addressing a fundamental challenge in deploying LLMs for reasoning tasks: the computational cost of scaling inference. SSR offers a potential pathway to improve the accuracy-efficiency trade-off, making it more practical to use LLMs for real-world applications, particularly in resource-constrained settings. The experimental results demonstrating substantial compute reduction with accuracy improvements are compelling. The results suggest practical utility.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The integration of SPM and SSD is well-motivated and addresses the limitations of previous methods.
    *   The training-free nature of the framework makes it readily applicable to existing LLMs without requiring fine-tuning.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, covering diverse mathematical benchmarks and comparing against relevant baselines.
    *   The compute-efficiency metric (Normalized FLOPs) provides a hardware-independent comparison of different inference strategies.
    *   The ablations studies provide further validation of the effects of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The creation of strategy pools can be a task in itself.
    *   While the paper claims low effort for creating a strategy pool, the process still requires some manual steps (prompt generation, verification), raising potential scalability concerns for new problem domains.
    *   The performance gains on AIME2024 are less pronounced, potentially indicating a limitation of the draft model's capability for extremely complex problems or limitations of the selected strategy pool.
    *   The choice of specific draft and target models (QwQ-32B and DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B) is fixed in the experiments; more extensive exploration across different model architectures could strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The performance depends heavily on how good a draft can the smaller LM output, and how well the larger LM can distinguish and revise the errors introduced by the draft model.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in efficient LLM inference and reasoning. The idea of step-level speculative decoding and selective parallel scaling could inspire new approaches for accelerating LLMs in other domains. If proven robust across different tasks and models, SSR could become a valuable tool for deploying LLMs in practice.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field by addressing a crucial issue in LLM deployment - inference efficiency. The novelty lies in the *specific* integration of existing techniques, tailored for multi-step reasoning, which leads to empirically validated gains. While the manual effort for creating the strategy pool and the model-dependency of the draft-target collaboration are limitations, the training-free and ready-to-use nature of SSR makes it attractive. The results, especially on MATH-500 and LiveMathBench, showing substantial compute reduction with even accuracy improvement, are compelling. The comprehensive evaluation with the normalized FLOPS metric adds rigor. Taking into account these strengths and weaknesses, the paper warrants a good score that acknowledges its contribution to the field while acknowledging the room for further improvements.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### FlowKV: Enhancing Multi-Turn Conversational Coherence in LLMs via Isolated Key-Value Cache Management
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15347v1)
- **Authors**: Xiang Liu, Hong Chen, Xuming Hu, Xiaowen Chu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in multi-turn conversational applications, where the management of the Key-Value (KV) Cache presents a significant bottleneck. The linear growth of the KV Cache with dialogue history imposes substantial computational costs, and existing eviction strategies often degrade performance by repeatedly compressing early conversational context, leading to information loss and context forgetting. This paper introduces FlowKV, a novel \textbf{multi-turn isolation mechanism} for KV Cache management, which can be applied to any KV Cache compression method without training. FlowKV's core innovation is a multi-turn isolation mechanism that preserves the accumulated compressed KV cache from past turns. Compression is then strategically applied only to the newly generated KV pairs of the latest completed turn, effectively preventing the re-compression of older context and thereby mitigating catastrophic forgetting. Our results demonstrate that FlowKV consistently and significantly outperforms baseline strategies in maintaining instruction-following accuracy and user preference retention from 10.90\% to 75.40\%, particularly in later conversational turns.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "FlowKV: Enhancing Multi-Turn Conversational Coherence in LLMs via Isolated Key-Value Cache Management" addresses the challenge of managing the KV cache in Large Language Models (LLMs) within multi-turn conversational settings.  The authors observe that traditional KV cache eviction strategies can lead to information loss and context forgetting due to repeated compression of early conversational context. They introduce FlowKV, a novel multi-turn isolation mechanism that preserves the accumulated compressed KV cache from previous turns, applying compression only to the newly generated KV pairs of the latest turn.  The authors demonstrate that FlowKV consistently outperforms baseline strategies in maintaining instruction-following accuracy and user preference retention, particularly in later conversational turns. The approach is training-free and compatible with existing KV cache compression methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of isolating and preserving the KV cache from previous turns while compressing the current turn's cache is relatively novel. It directly tackles a specific problem in multi-turn dialogue systems related to context degradation under compression. The simplicity and the training-free nature of FlowKV are appealing.
*   **Significance:**  The problem addressed is significant. KV cache management is a key bottleneck in deploying LLMs, and the performance degradation in multi-turn conversations is a practical concern. Demonstrating a method that mitigates this issue without significant computational overhead has practical value. The improvements shown in instruction following and preference retention are compelling.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and concise explanation of the problem and the proposed solution.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate a consistent and significant improvement over baseline strategies.  The detailed ablation studies and visualizations (especially the attention heatmap) provide valuable insights.
    *   The training-free aspect of FlowKV makes it easily adaptable to existing KV cache compression methods.
    *   The paper provides sufficient details to understand and potentially reproduce the results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper's evaluation, while comprehensive, is limited to only two models, LLaMA-3.1-8B and Qwen-2.5-7B, and two datasets, Multi-IF and PrefEval. Evaluating FlowKV on a wider range of models and datasets would strengthen the generalizability of the results.
    *   The improvement achieved is somewhat dependent on the base compression algorithm.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of LLM efficiency and multi-turn dialogue. The problem is well-defined, the solution is novel and effective, and the experimental results support the claims. While further validation with more models and datasets is desirable, the current evidence is sufficient to demonstrate the potential of FlowKV. Therefore, the contributions warrant a score on the higher end.

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### NL-Debugging: Exploiting Natural Language as an Intermediate Representation for Code Debugging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15356v1)
- **Authors**: Weiming Zhang, Qingyao Li, Xinyi Dai, Jizheng Chen, Kounianhua Du, Weinan Zhang, Weiwen Liu, Yasheng Wang, Ruiming Tang, Yong Yu
- **Abstract**: Debugging is a critical aspect of LLM's coding ability. Early debugging efforts primarily focused on code-level analysis, which often falls short when addressing complex programming errors that require a deeper understanding of algorithmic logic. Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have shifted attention toward leveraging natural language reasoning to enhance code-related tasks. However, two fundamental questions remain unanswered: What type of natural language format is most effective for debugging tasks? And what specific benefits does natural language reasoning bring to the debugging process? In this paper, we introduce NL-DEBUGGING, a novel framework that employs natural language as an intermediate representation to improve code debugging. By debugging at a natural language level, we demonstrate that NL-DEBUGGING outperforms traditional debugging methods and enables a broader modification space through direct refinement guided by execution feedback. Our findings highlight the potential of natural language reasoning to advance automated code debugging and address complex programming challenges.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "NL-Debugging: Exploiting Natural Language as an Intermediate Representation for Code Debugging":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NL-DEBUGGING, a novel framework that leverages natural language (NL) as an intermediate representation for debugging code generated by large language models (LLMs).  The framework operates in three phases: backtranslation (converting buggy code to NL), natural language refinement (debugging the NL representation using execution feedback), and regeneration (generating corrected code from the refined NL). The authors conduct experiments using APPS and Codeforces datasets, comparing NL-DEBUGGING against other code debugging methods. They investigate various aspects, including the effectiveness of different NL formats (sketches, pseudocode, key points), the benefits of natural language reasoning, and the role of execution feedback. The results suggest that NL-DEBUGGING, especially when using sketches as the intermediate representation, outperforms traditional code-level debugging methods and that the effectiveness of NL lies in providing a broader modification space, enabling more diverse corrections.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using natural language as an *intermediate representation* for debugging is relatively novel, especially compared to previous approaches that focused on code-level analysis or direct code manipulation by LLMs. The framework itself, with its backtranslation, refinement, and regeneration steps, is also a well-defined contribution. The systematic investigation of different NL formats is also a useful and less explored angle.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: the difficulty of debugging code generated by LLMs, particularly when dealing with logical errors. The findings suggest that NL-DEBUGGING can overcome some of the limitations of code-level debugging, opening up new avenues for automated code repair and iterative refinement. This can significantly improve the reliability of LLM-generated code.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined framework:** NL-DEBUGGING is presented as a clear, structured approach with distinct phases.
    *   **Systematic experimentation:** The paper thoroughly explores different aspects of the framework through a series of research questions (RQs) and controlled experiments.
    *   **Empirical evidence:** The experimental results on standard datasets (APPS and Codeforces) provide solid support for the claims.  The analysis comparing different NL formats is particularly insightful.
    *   **Addresses a significant problem:** The research tackles the challenging task of improving the quality of code generated by LLMs.
    *   **Good ablation studies**: The paper tests the importance of different components, such as feedback and NL-NL vs. NL-C approaches.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity & Practicality:**  The framework introduces an extra layer of abstraction (NL), potentially increasing complexity. The computational cost for backtranslation and regeneration should be compared to other methods. The framework does provide more modification space, but depending on the nature of the code, can introduce extra complexity as well.
    *   **Over-reliance on NL quality:** The success of NL-DEBUGGING heavily depends on the quality of the backtranslation and the refinement steps. If the initial NL representation is flawed or the refinement process is ineffective, the entire process could fail. The robustness of the system to noisy or ambiguous NL representations should be further investigated.
    *   **Limited Scope of Debugging:** The paper primarily focuses on algorithmic errors and does not extensively discuss NL-debugging's capability on other error types in programming, such as syntax errors, memory leaks, or concurrency issues.
    *   **Lack of Comparison on Efficiency**: While NL-DEBUGGING improves performance, the study could benefit from quantitative analysis of the framework's efficiency (e.g., runtime) compared to traditional debugging methods.

*   **Potential Influence:**  This paper could significantly influence research in automated code generation and debugging.  It encourages further exploration of NL as an intermediate representation, the use of sketches for capturing code logic, and the integration of execution feedback with NL reasoning.  The approach could also be adapted for other code-related tasks, such as code understanding and maintenance.

*Justification of the Score*
Overall, this is a sound contribution. The weaknesses are not fatal, and the contributions are clearly articulated. While it is likely that improvements to this framework will occur in later work (as is typical for most research), the initial concept provides a foundation for future improvements.
**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AI vs. Human Judgment of Content Moderation: LLM-as-a-Judge and Ethics-Based Response Refusals
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15365v1)
- **Authors**: Stefan Pasch
