**Justification:** While the paper presents interesting insights and is well-written, the limited experiments, missing theoretical validation, and lack of thorough validation limit the overall impact. This paper is valuable to have in the literature, as well as a valuable starting point.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluate Bias without Manual Test Sets: A Concept Representation Perspective for LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15524v1)
- **Authors**: Lang Gao, Kaiyang Wan, Wei Liu, Chenxi Wang, Zirui Song, Zixiang Xu, Yanbo Wang, Veselin Stoyanov, Xiuying Chen
- **Abstract**: Bias in Large Language Models (LLMs) significantly undermines their reliability and fairness. We focus on a common form of bias: when two reference concepts in the model's concept space, such as sentiment polarities (e.g., "positive" and "negative"), are asymmetrically correlated with a third, target concept, such as a reviewing aspect, the model exhibits unintended bias. For instance, the understanding of "food" should not skew toward any particular sentiment. Existing bias evaluation methods assess behavioral differences of LLMs by constructing labeled data for different social groups and measuring model responses across them, a process that requires substantial human effort and captures only a limited set of social concepts. To overcome these limitations, we propose BiasLens, a test-set-free bias analysis framework based on the structure of the model's vector space. BiasLens combines Concept Activation Vectors (CAVs) with Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to extract interpretable concept representations, and quantifies bias by measuring the variation in representational similarity between the target concept and each of the reference concepts. Even without labeled data, BiasLens shows strong agreement with traditional bias evaluation metrics (Spearman correlation r > 0.85). Moreover, BiasLens reveals forms of bias that are difficult to detect using existing methods. For example, in simulated clinical scenarios, a patient's insurance status can cause the LLM to produce biased diagnostic assessments. Overall, BiasLens offers a scalable, interpretable, and efficient paradigm for bias discovery, paving the way for improving fairness and transparency in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of evaluating bias in Large Language Models (LLMs). It argues that existing methods rely on manual test sets, which are labor-intensive and capture only a limited set of social concepts. The paper introduces BIASLENS, a test-set-free framework that analyzes bias by examining the structure of the LLM's vector space. BIASLENS combines Concept Activation Vectors (CAVs) with Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to extract interpretable concept representations and then quantifies bias by measuring the representational similarity between a target concept and reference concepts. The paper demonstrates that BIASLENS aligns well with traditional bias evaluation metrics and can reveal new, subtle forms of bias.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the test-set-free approach. Existing bias evaluation methods rely heavily on creating labeled datasets, which is a significant bottleneck. Shifting the focus to internal representations within the LLM's vector space is a worthwhile direction. The combination of CAVs and SAEs, while individually not novel, is a reasonable and logical approach to extracting interpretable concept representations. The geometric alignment bias measurement is also relatively straightforward, yet effective.

* **Significance:** The significance of this paper hinges on its potential to make bias evaluation more scalable, efficient, and interpretable. By eliminating the reliance on manual test sets, BIASLENS opens the door to more comprehensive and automated bias discovery. This could be particularly valuable in under-resourced scenarios where creating high-quality labeled data is infeasible. The framework's ability to uncover biases related to patient insurance status, a previously difficult-to-detect bias, highlights its potential. The interpretability aspect is also crucial, as it allows for a better understanding of the underlying causes of bias in LLMs.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Test-set-free Approach:** The most compelling strength is the elimination of manual test set construction, addressing a key limitation of existing bias evaluation methods.
    *   **Interpretability:** The use of SAEs to extract interpretable concept representations is a positive aspect, allowing for a better understanding of the biases being identified.
    *   **Strong Correlation with Existing Metrics:** The fact that BIASLENS correlates well with existing bias evaluation metrics (Spearman correlation r > 0.85) provides validation for the approach.
    *   **Discovery of New Biases:** Uncovering previously unreported biases, such as those related to patient insurance status, demonstrates the utility of the framework.
    *   **Automation and efficiency:** As it mentionned, the BIASLENS can achieve approximately a 50x speedup in both dataset construction and testing.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4 for Prompt Generation and SAE Classification:** The method relies on GPT-4 to generate positive probe data for CAV training, and to classify SAE features as concept-related. It may be difficult to reproduce if GPT-4 is unavailable.
    *   **Choice of LLM's and SAEs:** It is not clear if the model/sae selection will have some influence on the performance of BIASLENS.
    *   **Limited Experimentation with Diverse LLMs:** While the paper evaluates BIASLENS on three LLMs (Gemma 2 2B, Gemma 2 9B, Llama 3.1 8B), more extensive testing with a wider range of models is needed to establish the robustness of the approach.
    *   **Case Study Limitations:** The illustration of concept representation extraction (Figure 3) relies on a single case study. A more comprehensive evaluation across different concepts would strengthen the claims.

* **Impact:**
    The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM bias evaluation by providing a more scalable and interpretable alternative to existing methods. It could lead to the development of more robust bias mitigation techniques and contribute to the creation of fairer and more transparent LLMs.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM bias evaluation by introducing a test-set-free approach. It offers a promising framework that has the potential to overcome the limitations of existing methods and enable more comprehensive bias discovery. However, the score is not higher due to some reliance on GPT-4. Also, there is more work needed in exploring the effects of different kinds of model or sae selection on BIASLENS. Further experimentation with diverse LLMs and concept types is necessary to fully establish the robustness and generalizability of the approach.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Short-Range Dependency Effects on Transformer Instability and a Decomposed Attention Solution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15548v1)
- **Authors**: Suvadeep Hajra
- **Abstract**: Transformer language models have driven significant progress across various fields, including natural language processing and computer vision. A central component of these models is the self-attention (SA) mechanism, which learns rich vector representations of tokens by modeling their relationships with others in a sequence. However, despite extensive research, transformers continue to suffer from training instability -- often manifesting as spikes or divergence in the training loss during a run. In this work, we identify one source of this instability: SA's limited ability to capture short-range dependencies, especially in tasks like language modeling, where almost every token heavily relies on its nearby neighbors. This limitation causes the pre-softmax logits of SA to grow rapidly, destabilizing training. To address this, we propose decomposing the SA into local (short-range) and global (long-range) attention heads. This decomposed attention, referred to as Long Short-attention (LS-attention), mitigates logit explosion and results in more stable training compared to an equivalent multi-head self-attention (MHSA). Empirical comparisons with two alternative training stabilization methods show that LS-attention reduces the validation perplexity to nearly 2/5 of that achieved by one method and reaches a similar perplexity as the other method using only 1/20 of the GPU hours. Additionally, our experiments demonstrate that LS-attention reduces inference latency by up to 36% compared to a state-of-the-art implementation of equivalent MHSA.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "Short-Range Dependency Effects on Transformer Instability and a Decomposed Attention Solution" identifies the inability of standard self-attention (SA) mechanisms to effectively model short-range dependencies in long sequences as a key cause of training instability in transformer models.  The authors argue that this limitation leads to logit explosion and propose a novel decomposed attention mechanism called Long Short-attention (LS-attention). LS-attention divides attention heads into local (short-range) and global (long-range) components. They demonstrate empirically that LS-attention mitigates logit explosion, improves training stability, and achieves competitive performance compared to existing stabilization techniques, while also offering computational efficiency. The paper includes synthetic experiments, language modeling experiments using the PG-19 dataset, and ablations to support their claims.  They also show improved inference time with LS-Attention.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of decomposing the attention mechanism into short-range and long-range components isn't entirely new. Prior work has explored local attention and sparse attention variations, but the *specific combination* and *motivation* presented here are reasonably novel. The explicit connection to logit explosion and training instability due to the limitations of full self-attention in modeling *dense* short-range dependencies is a significant contribution. The way they frame the problem, showing that full attention is low-rank but that some problems require a full-rank attention matrix and that the model has to compensate for this by increasing logits, is novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the practical benefits and improved understanding of transformer behavior. The ability to stabilize training and reduce computational costs makes LS-attention a potentially valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working with large language models. Further, by explaining the underlying reason for instability the authors allow other researchers to better understand and possibly solve problems related to stability in language model training. The observed improvement in inference speed has practical implications for model deployment.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of training instability and provides a well-reasoned hypothesis about its cause (SA's limitations in modeling short-range dependencies).
    *   **Well-Designed Solution:** The LS-attention mechanism is a logical and relatively simple solution that addresses the identified problem.
    *   **Strong Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents thorough empirical evidence, including synthetic experiments, comparison against baselines, and ablation studies, to support its claims. The experiments using FP32 provide a solid baseline against which to measure LS-attention's effectiveness, as a means of stabilization. The experiments are consistent and the data presented is clear.
    *   **Practical Benefits:** The paper demonstrates the practical benefits of LS-attention in terms of training stability, performance, and efficiency.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the PG-19 dataset is a good choice for evaluating long-range dependencies, it would be beneficial to test LS-attention on a wider range of datasets and tasks. This would help to assess its generalizability and robustness. Further, though the PG-19 dataset has documents, it's not clear whether it is optimized for local relationships, and this would be a useful analysis.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of LS-attention might be sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters, such as the number of local and global attention heads and the attention span for local heads. The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of hyperparameter tuning.
    *   **Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides a compelling intuitive explanation, a more formal theoretical analysis of the properties of LS-attention would strengthen its contribution.
    *   **Comparison to other Methods:** More recent work has explored ways of stabilizing the training of language models. It would be useful to see a comparison of these new methods and LS-Attention.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more stable and efficient transformer models, particularly for tasks involving long sequences and dense local dependencies. The concept of decomposing attention could inspire further research into attention mechanisms.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel perspective on transformer instability and offers a practical solution with strong empirical support. While the idea of decomposed attention has been explored in some form previously, the paper's framing of the problem, the specific implementation of LS-attention, and the demonstration of its benefits are significant contributions. The weaknesses, such as the limited scope of evaluation and lack of deeper theoretical analysis, prevent it from achieving a higher score. However, its clarity, strong experimental results, and practical implications make it a valuable addition to the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Social Bias in Popular Question-Answering Benchmarks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15553v1)
- **Authors**: Angelie Kraft, Judith Simon, Sonja Schimmler
- **Abstract**: Question-answering (QA) and reading comprehension (RC) benchmarks are essential for assessing the capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in retrieving and reproducing knowledge. However, we demonstrate that popular QA and RC benchmarks are biased and do not cover questions about different demographics or regions in a representative way, potentially due to a lack of diversity of those involved in their creation. We perform a qualitative content analysis of 30 benchmark papers and a quantitative analysis of 20 respective benchmark datasets to learn (1) who is involved in the benchmark creation, (2) how social bias is addressed or prevented, and (3) whether the demographics of the creators and annotators correspond to particular biases in the content. Most analyzed benchmark papers provided insufficient information regarding the stakeholders involved in benchmark creation, particularly the annotators. Notably, just one of the benchmark papers explicitly reported measures taken to address social representation issues. Moreover, the data analysis revealed gender, religion, and geographic biases across a wide range of encyclopedic, commonsense, and scholarly benchmarks. More transparent and bias-aware QA and RC benchmark creation practices are needed to facilitate better scrutiny and incentivize the development of fairer LLMs.
- **Summary**: This paper systematically analyzes social biases in popular question-answering (QA) and reading comprehension (RC) benchmarks used for evaluating Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors conduct a qualitative content analysis of 30 benchmark papers and a quantitative analysis of 20 datasets to investigate the stakeholders involved, bias prevention measures, and the correlation between creator demographics and content biases. Their findings reveal a lack of transparency regarding annotator involvement, insufficient bias mitigation strategies, and prominent gender, religious, and geographic biases in widely used benchmarks. The authors argue that these biases incentivize the development of unfair LLMs and contribute to epistemic injustice.

**Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Analysis:** The paper presents a well-structured and systematic analysis, combining both qualitative and quantitative methods to investigate biases in QA/RC benchmarks.
    *   **Identifying Specific Biases:** It identifies concrete examples of gender, religious, and geographic biases within datasets, which provides a tangible understanding of the problem.
    *   **Addressing Transparency:** Highlights a critical lack of transparency related to annotator recruitment and demographics, calling for more open and inclusive practices.
    *   **Connecting to Broader Impact:** The paper effectively connects the biases in benchmarks to the potential for perpetuating societal inequalities through LLMs, emphasizing the real-world implications of biased evaluation metrics.
    *   **Extensive References:** Demonstrates strong prior work awareness with comprehensive citations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Causal Analysis:** The study identifies correlations between creator demographics and dataset biases, but it does not establish a direct causal relationship. Statistical significance is not highlighted, and the study is limited by the difficulty of accessing sufficient data on benchmark creators.
    *   **Lack of Detailed Mitigation Strategies:** While the paper effectively identifies problems, it provides limited concrete suggestions for mitigating these biases in future benchmark creation. The analysis remains largely descriptive.
    *   **Reliance on Wikidata:** Though Wikidata provides valuable metadata, using it as a sole source could be subject to bias in the data itself; information is often incomplete, skewed towards western perspectives, or poorly maintained.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper significantly contributes to the ongoing discussion about fairness and bias in AI by focusing on a crucial, yet often overlooked, aspect: the benchmarks used to evaluate LLMs. This differs from prior work predominantly focusing on training data biases.
    *   It provides an empirical basis for concerns about the representativeness of these benchmarks. This systematic approach distinguishes it from other, more anecdotal critiques of benchmark biases.

    *   While much literature points out the downstream harms of biased models, this paper highlights biases in the very *measuring sticks* of AI progress.

**Justification:**

The paper's strengths are considerable. Its systematic approach and tangible bias identification make it valuable within the AI fairness and ethics fields. However, the primary weakness is the limited causal analysis and lack of concrete mitigation strategies. Although the limitations prevent the paper from receiving a rating on the very high end of the spectrum, the significance of the work in focusing on bias within benchmarks—a often overlooked but crucial area—make it impactful.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DayDreamer at CQs-Gen 2025: Generating Critical Questions through Argument Scheme Completion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15554v1)
- **Authors**: Wendi Zhou, Ameer Saadat-Yazdi, Nadin Kökciyan
- **Abstract**: Critical questions are essential resources to provoke critical thinking when encountering an argumentative text. We present our system for the Critical Questions Generation (CQs-Gen) Shared Task at ArgMining 2025. Our approach leverages large language models (LLMs) with chain-of-thought prompting to generate critical questions guided by Walton's argumentation schemes. For each input intervention, we conversationally prompt LLMs to instantiate the corresponding argument scheme template to first obtain structured arguments, and then generate relevant critical questions. Following this, we rank all the available critical questions by prompting LLMs to select the top 3 most helpful questions based on the original intervention text. This combination of structured argumentation theory and step-by-step reasoning enables the generation of contextually relevant and diverse critical questions. Our pipeline achieves competitive performance in the final test set, showing its potential to foster critical thinking given argumentative text and detect missing or uninformed claims. Code available at \href{https://git.ecdf.ed.ac.uk/s2236454/DayDreamer-CQs-Gen}{DayDreamer}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper describes the "DayDreamer" system, designed for the CQs-Gen 2025 shared task on generating critical questions (CQs) for argumentative texts.  The system leverages large language models (LLMs) and chain-of-thought prompting, incorporating Walton's argumentation schemes.  The pipeline consists of three main stages: Argument Extraction, Critical Question Generation, and Ranking. The system prompts LLMs to instantiate argument scheme templates to obtain structured arguments, generate relevant critical questions, and then rank those questions based on their helpfulness given the original intervention text.  Experiments using GPT-40-mini and LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct are presented, with GPT-40-mini achieving the best performance in terms of generating more helpful and fewer invalid/unhelpful questions. The authors also explore pipeline optimization techniques on the validation set, including conversational prompting, sorting scheme names, and removing inaccurate scheme templates.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Integration of Argumentation Theory:**  The paper's core strength lies in its principled approach, grounding CQ generation in argumentation theory, specifically Walton's argumentation schemes. This provides a structured way to generate questions, moving beyond generic question generation.
    * **Chain-of-Thought and LLMs:** The use of chain-of-thought prompting and LLMs is a natural fit for this task. It allows the system to reason step-by-step, potentially leading to more relevant and nuanced CQs.
    * **Systematic Pipeline:** The three-stage pipeline is clearly defined and modular. This allows for independent optimization of each stage.
    * **Experimental Evaluation:**  The paper includes experimental results with different LLMs and ablation studies on the validation set, providing some insight into the effectiveness of various components.  The performance comparisons on the test set (4th place) offer a relative measure of success.
    * **Detailed Ablation Study:** The detailed ablation study on the validation set provides very valuable insights into the quality of the scheme templates, and their impact on the results of the pipeline. This offers valuable insight in future development of similar approaches.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty:** While the combination of argumentation schemes and LLMs is sensible, the overall approach is incremental rather than groundbreaking. Similar approaches to related tasks (e.g., argument mining, stance detection) have used similar techniques.
    * **LLM Dependency:** The system is heavily reliant on the capabilities of LLMs. This means its performance is tied to the current state-of-the-art in LLMs, and it may be susceptible to issues like hallucination or biases in the LLMs.
    * **Lack of Detail on CQ Ranking:** The ranking stage is described, but more detail on the specific ranking criteria used in the prompt would be beneficial. Also, the ranking of critical questions based solely on helpfulness may not be the most optimal approach.
    * **"ER" Schemes Issue:** The discovery and subsequent handling of the undefined "ER" schemes highlights a potential limitation. It raises questions about the robustness of the approach to inconsistencies or errors in the underlying argumentation scheme taxonomy.
    * **Reproducibility Issues:** Due to the reliance on LLMs, there could be reproducibility issues. LLMs are rapidly evolving, and accessing older models, such as GPT-40-mini, may not be possible.

* **Significance:**
    * The paper demonstrates a practical application of argumentation theory in the context of automated critical question generation.
    * The experimental results provide some empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the approach.
    * The analysis of different components and the "ER" schemes issue offer valuable insights for future research in this area.

* **Overall:**

The paper presents a reasonably sound system for generating critical questions based on argumentation schemes and LLMs. However, the approach is not fundamentally novel and relies heavily on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs. The detailed evaluation and the insights gained from the ablation studies are valuable contributions, but the limitations related to LLM dependency and the issues with "ER" schemes reduce the overall impact.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a decent but incremental advance. While the combination of argumentation schemes and LLMs is a logical approach, its overall novelty is limited. The lack of truly groundbreaking advancements, reliance on LLMs, and reliance on specific LLMs such as GPT-40-mini limits its appeal. The weaknesses discussed above (dependency on LLMs, ranking of CQs) justify the assigned score. However, the paper does demonstrate that the approach of combining argumentative schemes and LLMs in a structured and well defined pipeline can yield reasonable results.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Beyond Classification: Evaluating Diffusion Denoised Smoothing for Security-Utility Trade off
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15594v1)
- **Authors**: Yury Belousov, Brian Pulfer, Vitaliy Kinakh, Slava Voloshynovskiy
- **Abstract**: While foundation models demonstrate impressive performance across various tasks, they remain vulnerable to adversarial inputs. Current research explores various approaches to enhance model robustness, with Diffusion Denoised Smoothing emerging as a particularly promising technique. This method employs a pretrained diffusion model to preprocess inputs before model inference. Yet, its effectiveness remains largely unexplored beyond classification. We aim to address this gap by analyzing three datasets with four distinct downstream tasks under three different adversarial attack algorithms. Our findings reveal that while foundation models maintain resilience against conventional transformations, applying high-noise diffusion denoising to clean images without any distortions significantly degrades performance by as high as 57%. Low-noise diffusion settings preserve performance but fail to provide adequate protection across all attack types. Moreover, we introduce a novel attack strategy specifically targeting the diffusion process itself, capable of circumventing defenses in the low-noise regime. Our results suggest that the trade-off between adversarial robustness and performance remains a challenge to be addressed.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of Diffusion Denoised Smoothing (DDS) as a defense mechanism against adversarial attacks on Vision Foundation Models (VFMs).  The authors examine the trade-off between adversarial robustness and performance across various downstream tasks (classification, segmentation, depth estimation, and image retrieval) and different adversarial attack strategies. The core findings are: 1) VFMs are highly vulnerable to adversarial attacks despite being robust to common distortions. 2) High-noise DDS offers substantial defense but significantly degrades clean performance. 3) Low-noise DDS preserves performance but is vulnerable to adaptive attacks, particularly those incorporating diffusion during the attack generation. The paper introduces a novel attack targeting the diffusion process, further highlighting the challenges of balancing robustness and performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive evaluation of DDS across a range of downstream tasks beyond simple classification. While DDS itself isn't a novel technique, applying it to modern VFMs and analyzing the robustness-performance trade-offs across diverse applications and attacks is a valuable contribution. The introduction of an attack specifically targeting the diffusion process is a useful addition, even if its efficacy might be limited to specific scenarios. However, the analysis and attack methods are based on relatively standard adversarial attack techniques (PGD, MI-FGSM, SIA), which limits the novelty in terms of adversarial methods.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical insights into the limitations of DDS as a defense for VFMs.  It highlights a critical challenge: achieving meaningful adversarial robustness without sacrificing substantial performance, particularly in security-critical applications. The finding that VFMs are surprisingly vulnerable to adversarial perturbations, even after denoising, despite their robustness to other corruptions, is an important takeaway for practitioners deploying these models. The paper provides a systematic assessment that encourages future research to focus on more robust VFMs rather than post-hoc defense mechanisms.  The detailed evaluation across various tasks and attack types makes the findings practically relevant. However, while it points out limitations, the paper doesn't offer any new techniques for improving either robustness or clean performance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Examines DDS across a variety of downstream tasks, VFMs, and attack methods.
*   **Practical Relevance:** Addresses a crucial issue in deploying VFMs in real-world applications where security is paramount.
*   **Clear Insights:**  The paper reveals key trade-offs and limitations of DDS, providing valuable guidance for future research.
*   **Reproducibility:** Promises to make the codebase available, promoting future research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Attack Methods:**  The core adversarial attack methods are standard.
*   **No Novel Defense Mechanisms:**  The paper mainly focuses on evaluating existing methods.
*   **Incremental Contribution:** The findings confirm known vulnerabilities of existing defense mechanisms with more modern foundation models and tasks, but do not propose solutions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a useful, thorough, and practically relevant contribution by highlighting the limitations of Diffusion Denoised Smoothing when applied to modern vision foundation models across various downstream tasks. However, it mainly confirms existing knowledge rather than introducing entirely new attack or defense mechanisms. The impact and novelty are, therefore, limited. The paper provides a clear and systematic analysis, but the findings are incremental.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### From Problem-Solving to Teaching Problem-Solving: Aligning LLMs with Pedagogy using Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15607v1)
- **Authors**: David Dinucu-Jianu, Jakub Macina, Nico Daheim, Ido Hakimi, Iryna Gurevych, Mrinmaya Sachan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) can transform education, but their optimization for direct question-answering often undermines effective pedagogy which requires strategically withholding answers. To mitigate this, we propose an online reinforcement learning (RL)-based alignment framework that can quickly adapt LLMs into effective tutors using simulated student-tutor interactions by emphasizing pedagogical quality and guided problem-solving over simply giving away answers. We use our method to train a 7B parameter tutor model without human annotations which reaches similar performance to larger proprietary models like LearnLM. We introduce a controllable reward weighting to balance pedagogical support and student solving accuracy, allowing us to trace the Pareto frontier between these two objectives. Our models better preserve reasoning capabilities than single-turn SFT baselines and can optionally enhance interpretability through thinking tags that expose the model's instructional planning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an online Reinforcement Learning (RL) framework to align Large Language Models (LLMs) with pedagogical principles for tutoring. It addresses the limitations of existing approaches, such as supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and preference-based RL, by training a 7B parameter tutor model through simulated student-tutor interactions. The framework uses reward functions that emphasize pedagogical quality and guided problem-solving over simply providing answers. A key contribution is the introduction of controllable reward weighting, enabling a trade-off between pedagogical support and student accuracy, tracing a Pareto frontier between these objectives. The models preserve reasoning capabilities better than SFT baselines and optionally enhance interpretability using "thinking tags" to expose the model's instructional planning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using online RL for pedagogical alignment of LLMs, particularly using synthetic student-tutor interactions *without human annotations*, is a valuable step. The ability to explicitly control the trade-off between solution accuracy and pedagogical soundness using controllable reward weighting is also innovative. The addition of "thinking tags" to enhance interpretability is a nice, though perhaps less substantial, contribution.
*   **Significance:** The results showcase that a relatively small (7B) RL-trained model can achieve performance comparable to larger proprietary models like LearnLM. This is significant as it reduces the reliance on large models and expensive human annotations. Demonstrating that reasoning abilities are preserved, unlike SocraticLM, is also impactful.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Cost-effectiveness:** The synthetic data pipeline significantly reduces the need for expensive human annotations, a common bottleneck in educational AI.
    *   **Controllability:** The ability to adjust the balance between pedagogical support and answer accuracy provides valuable flexibility for different learning scenarios.
    *   **Reasoning preservation:** Maintaining performance on standard reasoning benchmarks (MMLU, GSM8K, MATH) demonstrates that the pedagogical alignment doesn't come at the expense of fundamental skills.
    *   **Online RL:** The adoption of an online RL method where the model learns from its own dialogs helps mitigate issues related to exposure bias that prior off-policy methods can suffer from.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Synthetic Student Model:** The framework relies heavily on the quality of the simulated student model. While the student model used in the paper might be sufficient, its fidelity to real student behaviors is inherently limited. This reliance raises concerns about the model learning to "teach" to an artificial student instead of generalizing to real learners.
    *   **Limited Evaluation with Real Students:** The paper lacks evaluation with real-world student data. While the automatic metrics are valuable, ultimately, the effectiveness of a tutoring system must be assessed with human learners. The paper acknowledges this and points out the future scope of the same.
    *   **Scope of Problems:** The problems considered are mostly mathematics-related. The generalizability of the findings to other domains and more open-ended tasks is an open question.
    *  **Judge reliance:** Even with the use of model ensembles, the judge prompt method to evaluate the pedagogical quality can have some bias to certain conversations over the others, possibly leading to the RL method tuning the tutor model towards that bias.
*   **Potential Influence:** If the framework proves effective with real students, it could significantly lower the barriers to creating personalized AI tutors. The ability to control the pedagogical style and preserve reasoning abilities could lead to more effective and adaptable learning experiences.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper demonstrates a clever approach to address a significant problem in the educational AI space: aligning LLMs with effective pedagogical strategies. The online RL framework with synthetic data is a compelling solution for reducing costs and achieving a controllable trade-off between desired objectives. However, the limited evaluation with real students and the reliance on a synthetic student model are significant limitations. The synthetic student model reliance in particular is a central part of the design and its effectiveness is unknown to be similar to real students.

Given these considerations, a score of **7** is appropriate. The paper presents a novel and significant method but requires validation with real-world data to fully demonstrate its impact and potential.

Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LENS: Multi-level Evaluation of Multimodal Reasoning with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15616v1)
- **Authors**: Ruilin Yao, Bo Zhang, Jirui Huang, Xinwei Long, Yifang Zhang, Tianyu Zou, Yufei Wu, Shichao Su, Yifan Xu, Wenxi Zeng, Zhaoyu Yang, Guoyou Li, Shilan Zhang, Zichan Li, Yaxiong Chen, Shengwu Xiong, Peng Xu, Jiajun Zhang, Bowen Zhou, David Clifton, Luc Van Gool
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved significant advances in integrating visual and linguistic information, yet their ability to reason about complex and real-world scenarios remains limited. The existing benchmarks are usually constructed in the task-oriented manner without guarantee that different task samples come from the same data distribution, thus they often fall short in evaluating the synergistic effects of lower-level perceptual capabilities on higher-order reasoning. To lift this limitation, we contribute Lens, a multi-level benchmark with 3.4K contemporary images and 60K+ human-authored questions covering eight tasks and 12 daily scenarios, forming three progressive task tiers, i.e., perception, understanding, and reasoning. One feature is that each image is equipped with rich annotations for all tasks. Thus, this dataset intrinsically supports to evaluate MLLMs to handle image-invariable prompts, from basic perception to compositional reasoning. In addition, our images are manully collected from the social media, in which 53% were published later than Jan. 2025. We evaluate 15+ frontier MLLMs such as Qwen2.5-VL-72B, InternVL3-78B, GPT-4o and two reasoning models QVQ-72B-preview and Kimi-VL. These models are released later than Dec. 2024, and none of them achieve an accuracy greater than 60% in the reasoning tasks. Project page: https://github.com/Lens4MLLMs/lens. ICCV 2025 workshop page: https://lens4mllms.github.io/mars2-workshop-iccv2025/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LENS paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LENS, a multi-level benchmark designed to evaluate multimodal reasoning capabilities in large language models (MLLMs).  LENS focuses on assessing perception, understanding, and reasoning through a hierarchical structure with eight tasks spanning twelve daily scenarios.  A key feature is the rich annotation of each image for all tasks, facilitating consistent evaluation across different levels of reasoning. The dataset comprises 3.4K real-world images, many from 2025, and over 60K human-authored questions. The paper evaluates 15+ recent MLLMs and demonstrates that even the most advanced models struggle with reasoning tasks in LENS.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in several aspects:

    *   **Hierarchical Structure:** The explicit organization of tasks into perception, understanding, and reasoning levels is a valuable contribution, providing a structured way to analyze model strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Image-Invariance and Cross-Task Consistency:** The rich annotation of each image for all tasks is a unique feature.  This allows for controlled experiments where the image remains constant while the level of reasoning required changes, which distinguishes it from task-oriented benchmarks.
    *   **Realistic and Up-to-Date Data:** The focus on contemporary real-world images, with a significant portion from 2025, is a significant step forward, reducing the bias toward older datasets.
    *   **Focus on Synergistic Effects:**  The design of the benchmark explicitly aims to evaluate how lower-level perceptual abilities contribute to higher-order reasoning, addressing a significant gap in existing evaluations.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper directly addresses the limitations of existing benchmarks, which often fail to capture the synergistic effects of multi-level reasoning and use outdated or task-specific datasets.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The benchmark provides a comprehensive evaluation framework, allowing researchers to identify specific areas where MLLMs need improvement.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The focus on real-world scenarios makes the benchmark relevant to practical applications of MLLMs.
    *   **Challenging Benchmark:** The evaluation results demonstrate that LENS is a challenging benchmark, even for state-of-the-art MLLMs, suggesting that it will be a valuable resource for driving future research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly articulates the motivation, design, and evaluation of the LENS benchmark.
    *   The dataset creation and annotation process are described in detail, enhancing reproducibility.
    *   The experimental results provide valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations of current MLLMs.
    *   The analysis of synergistic effects is a significant contribution to the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset size, while substantial, could be larger to increase diversity and robustness.  While 3.4K images is more than many vision-language datasets, more would better stress-test the models.
    *   The evaluation is primarily focused on accuracy. While appropriate as a primary metric, supplementing it with qualitative analysis of the model's reasoning processes (e.g., error analysis) would further strengthen the findings.  This would also help demonstrate where improvements at different levels (perception vs. reasoning) are needed to improve overall performance.
    *   The annotation relies on human annotators. Though quality control measures are mentioned, there's always the potential for annotation bias or inconsistencies. Quantifying inter-annotator agreement would further bolster confidence in the data.

*   **Impact:** LENS has the potential to significantly impact the field of multimodal learning by providing a more rigorous and realistic evaluation framework. It will likely become a standard benchmark for assessing the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs. The focus on contemporary data and synergistic effects will encourage researchers to develop models that are better suited for real-world applications.

**Rationale for Score:**

LENS is a significant contribution because it directly addresses critical gaps in the evaluation of MLLMs. Its hierarchical structure, focus on image-invariance, realistic data, and synergistic effects are all valuable advancements. The evaluation results are compelling and highlight the limitations of current models. While the dataset size could be larger and additional qualitative analysis would strengthen the findings, the overall quality and impact of the work justify a high score.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DS-Bench: A Realistic Benchmark for Data Science Code Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15621v1)
- **Authors**: Shuyin Ouyang, Dong Huang, Jingwen Guo, Zeyu Sun, Qihao Zhu, Jie M. Zhang
- **Abstract**: We introduce DS-bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate large language models (LLMs) on complicated and realistic data science code generation tasks. DS-bench consists of 1,000 carefully constructed problems sourced from realistic problems from GitHub across ten widely used Python data science libraries. Compared to the current state-of-the-art benchmark DS-1000, DS-bench offers a more challenging and representative testbed, longer code solutions, more comprehensive data science libraries, clearer and better structured problem descriptions, and stronger test suites. To construct the DS-bench, we develop a robust pipeline that combines task scope selection, code construction, test case generation, and problem description synthesis. The process is paired with rigorous manual editing to ensure alignment and enhance evaluation reliability. Experimental result shows that DS-bench exhibits robust scaling behavior, where larger models systematically outperform smaller ones, validating its ability to distinguish model capabilities. The best LLM we test, GPT-4o, has a pass@1 of 0.202, indicating that LLMs still have a large room to improve for realistic data science code generation tasks. We believe DS-bench will serve as a rigorous and trustworthy foundation for advancing LLM-based data science programming.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DS-Bench: A Realistic Benchmark for Data Science Code Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DS-bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate large language models (LLMs) on the task of generating data science code.  DS-bench comprises 1,000 problems sourced from real-world GitHub repositories covering ten popular Python data science libraries.  The benchmark aims to address limitations of existing datasets like DS-1000 by providing more realistic and complex problems, longer code solutions, more comprehensive library coverage, better structured problem descriptions, and stronger test suites.  The authors describe a modular pipeline for constructing the benchmark, including task scope determination, code construction, test case generation, problem description synthesis, and manual editing.  The paper evaluates several state-of-the-art LLMs on DS-bench and shows that it presents a more challenging and discriminating testbed compared to DS-1000.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation of a more realistic and challenging benchmark for data science code generation. While DS-1000 existed, DS-bench addresses its shortcomings significantly. The enhanced pipeline for problem generation, focusing on GitHub repositories over Stack Overflow, leads to more complex scenarios. The increase in problem length and number of test cases directly addresses the limitations of previous benchmarks. The addition of Seaborn, Keras, and LightGBM is a welcome expansion of library coverage. However, the individual components of the problem generation pipeline (LLM prompting, AST parsing, test case generation) are not entirely novel in isolation.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work is substantial.  By providing a more rigorous and representative benchmark, DS-bench can drive progress in LLM-based data science programming. The experiments showing the scaling behavior of LLMs and the relatively low performance of even the best models (GPT-4o) on DS-bench highlight the remaining challenges and provide a clear target for future research. The benchmark's improved test suite and problem descriptions also contribute to more reliable evaluation of LLMs. Open-sourcing DS-bench ensures that it can be used and extended by the broader research community. By providing a more challenging and nuanced benchmark than DS-1000, this paper serves as a vital stepping stone for advancements in the field. A minor weakness is the limitation to Python and primarily unit-test based evaluations. Also, while GitHub is a valuable source of information, it is prone to containing a number of examples that are not high quality, which could have negative impacts on the performance of models trained on the dataset.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined and modular benchmark construction pipeline.
    *   Focus on realistic code from GitHub repositories.
    *   Comprehensive library coverage and well-structured problem descriptions.
    *   Stronger test suites with customizable configurations.
    *   Empirical evaluation of several state-of-the-art LLMs.
    *   Demonstration of clear scaling behavior and the limitations of current models.
    *   Open-sourced benchmark for community use and extension.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited to Python and predominantly unit-test based evaluation.
    *   Some simplifications in the problem generation pipeline (e.g., error handling).
    *   GitHub may not always be the source of the highest-quality examples.
    *   Some of the pipeline steps rely on LLMs, which could lead to inherent biases.
    *   Lack of evaluation across important dimensions of code quality, such as computational efficiency, coding style, and security.

*   **Potential Influence:** DS-bench has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for evaluating LLMs in data science, similar to how DS-1000 has been adopted. It can drive research toward developing more robust, reliable, and practical LLMs for data science programming.  The availability of the benchmark will likely spur innovation in code generation techniques and lead to the development of new metrics for evaluating code quality.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** DS-bench represents a significant improvement over existing benchmarks in data science code generation, providing a more realistic and challenging evaluation environment. It is well-designed, open-sourced, and supported by thorough experimental results. Its clear weaknesses, notably the Python limitation and evaluation metrics, present opportunities for future improvements. The benchmark's potential to drive progress in LLM-based data science programming warrants a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Can LLMs $\textit{understand}$ Math? -- Exploring the Pitfalls in Mathematical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15623v1)
- **Authors**: Tiasa Singha Roy, Aditeya Baral, Ayush Rajesh Jhaveri, Yusuf Baig
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) demonstrate considerable potential in various natural language tasks but face significant challenges in mathematical reasoning, particularly in executing precise, multi-step logic. However, current evaluation frameworks judge their performance solely based on accuracy, which only accounts for the final answer. This study explores these pitfalls by employing a novel evaluation framework. We propose an evaluation metric called the MAPLE score, which holistically quantifies reasoning misalignment by integrating error rates, redundancy, and validity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Can LLMs Understand Math? Exploring the Pitfalls in Mathematical Reasoning" addresses the limitations of evaluating Large Language Models (LLMs) solely based on final answer accuracy in mathematical reasoning tasks. It introduces a novel evaluation framework incorporating a metric called MAPLE (Mathematical Pitfalls and Logical Evaluation) score. MAPLE quantifies reasoning misalignment by integrating error rates, redundancy, and validity, using a multi-stage evaluation methodology involving LLM self-reflection and error clustering.  The framework is applied to several LLMs on the MATH dataset, revealing patterns of errors and limitations across different mathematical topics and difficulty levels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its proposed MAPLE score and associated evaluation framework. While self-reflection and error analysis techniques have been explored, integrating them into a holistic metric that accounts for both quantitative and qualitative aspects of mathematical reasoning is a worthwhile contribution. The use of clustered error labels to represent common reasoning failures offers more granular insight than just overall accuracy.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial issue in LLM evaluation: the over-reliance on final answer accuracy. By highlighting the importance of assessing the quality of reasoning steps, it contributes to a more nuanced understanding of LLM capabilities and limitations. Identifying specific error types and their prevalence across different mathematical topics allows for targeted improvements in LLM training and architecture. The topic of evaluating LLMs on complex mathematical problems is a significant and growing area of research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing evaluation methods.
    *   **Novel Metric:**  The MAPLE score provides a more comprehensive assessment of LLM mathematical reasoning than simple accuracy.
    *   **Multi-Stage Methodology:** The proposed evaluation methodology is well-defined and allows for a deeper understanding of LLM errors.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The experiments on the MATH dataset, using various LLMs, provide empirical support for the framework's effectiveness.
    *   **Error Analysis:** Identifying and classifying common error types provides valuable insights for improving LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Subjectivity in Error Labeling:** The process of clustering errors and assigning labels can be subjective, potentially introducing bias.
    *   **Reliance on LLM as Judge:** Using an LLM to identify errors (Judge LLM) can also introduce errors into the labeling process. While the paper claims that there is high alignment with human labels, this is an important consideration.
    *   **Limited Scale of Manual Validation:** The manual annotation for validation (105 examples) could be considered limited given the size and diversity of the MATH dataset. A larger validation set could provide more robust evidence of the accuracy of the Judge LLM's error predictions.
    *   **Complexity of MAPLE Score:** The MAPLE score, incorporating multiple factors (error rate, redundancy, validity), adds complexity to the evaluation process. Although aiming for a holistic perspective, it can be less intuitive to interpret than more straightforward accuracy metrics.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence how LLMs are evaluated for mathematical reasoning. The MAPLE score and associated framework provide a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners looking to develop more robust and reliable LLMs for mathematical tasks.  It encourages a shift from simply measuring accuracy to understanding the underlying reasoning processes and identifying areas for improvement.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some weaknesses regarding subjectivity and potential bias in error labeling, the overall contribution is significant. The proposed MAPLE score is a novel approach to evaluating the quality of mathematical reasoning by LLMs, addressing limitations of relying solely on final answer accuracy. The error analysis provides valuable insights into the challenges LLMs face in complex reasoning tasks.  The impact on the evaluation and future development of LLMs on complex reasoning problems is significant. However, the paper could benefit from a more rigorous validation of the error prediction done by the LLM judge by having more human labelers assessing a higher number of outputs from the different models evaluated. Taking all this into consideration, a score of 7.5 is an appropriate balance.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mechanistic Insights into Grokking from the Embedding Layer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15624v1)
- **Authors**: H. V. AlquBoj, Hilal AlQuabeh, Velibor Bojkovic, Munachiso Nwadike, Kentaro Inui
- **Abstract**: Grokking, a delayed generalization in neural networks after perfect training performance, has been observed in Transformers and MLPs, but the components driving it remain underexplored. We show that embeddings are central to grokking: introducing them into MLPs induces delayed generalization in modular arithmetic tasks, whereas MLPs without embeddings can generalize immediately. Our analysis identifies two key mechanisms: (1) Embedding update dynamics, where rare tokens stagnate due to sparse gradient updates and weight decay, and (2) Bilinear coupling, where the interaction between embeddings and downstream weights introduces saddle points and increases sensitivity to initialization. To confirm these mechanisms, we investigate frequency-aware sampling, which balances token updates by minimizing gradient variance, and embedding-specific learning rates, derived from the asymmetric curvature of the bilinear loss landscape. We prove that an adaptive learning rate ratio, \(\frac{\eta_E}{\eta_W} \propto \frac{\sigma_{\max}(E)}{\sigma_{\max}(W)} \cdot \frac{f_W}{f_E}\), mitigates bilinear coupling effects, accelerating convergence. Our methods not only improve grokking dynamics but also extend to broader challenges in Transformer optimization, where bilinear interactions hinder efficient training.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the grokking phenomenon (delayed generalization) in neural networks, focusing specifically on the role of the embedding layer in MLPs. The authors argue that embedding layers, when introduced into MLPs for modular arithmetic tasks, induce grokking behavior that isn't observed in MLPs without embeddings.  Their analysis identifies two key mechanisms: (1) **Embedding update dynamics**, where rare tokens stagnate due to sparse gradient updates and weight decay, and (2) **Bilinear coupling**, the interaction between embeddings and downstream weights introduces saddle points, increasing sensitivity to initialization. They propose solutions: frequency-aware sampling to balance token updates and embedding-specific learning rates (Adam-LR) that adaptively adjust learning rates to mitigate bilinear coupling effects.  The Adam-LR optimizer is shown to improve grokking dynamics and generalization. The paper also suggests connections between these findings and broader optimization challenges in Transformers, where bilinear interactions (e.g., in attention) can hinder efficient training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel perspective by emphasizing the role of the *embedding layer* as a key driver of grokking. While previous work has explored grokking from various angles, this component-specific focus is valuable. The observation that embeddings introduce grokking to MLPs on modular arithmetic tasks where it would otherwise not appear is itself a significant contribution. Also, the *identification of embedding update dynamics and bilinear coupling* as specific challenges are interesting and have value. Adam-LR provides an adaptive embedding-specific rate.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in: (1) Providing a more granular understanding of grokking by linking it to specific architectural components (embeddings) and their optimization dynamics. (2) Offering practical solutions (frequency-aware sampling, adaptive learning rates) that can improve generalization performance. (3) Drawing parallels between embedding dynamics in MLPs and optimization challenges in Transformers, suggesting that insights from simpler models can inform strategies for more complex architectures. (4) Demonstrating a connection between the properties in bilinear systems and the interplay between initialization, update frequency and curvature.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear identification of research question and hypotheses.
    *   Well-designed experiments to test the hypotheses.
    *   Rigorous analysis of the results, leading to meaningful conclusions.
    *   Convincing demonstration of the effectiveness of the proposed solutions.
    *   Good connections between findings and potential applications in other areas (Transformers).
    *   Detailed mathematical justifications of the methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the link to Transformers is suggested, a direct experimental validation in the context of Transformers could strengthen the claims. The paper primarily analyzes the phenomena within the MLP framework; it would be good to test in more complex models.
    *   The experiments are focused on relatively simple algorithmic tasks. While these tasks are useful for controlled analysis, it is not immediately clear how well these insights generalize to real-world datasets.
    *   The improvement in performance is marginal and not significant, specially on figures 4 and 6.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in grokking and neural network optimization. It encourages a more component-centric approach to understanding generalization and highlights the importance of considering the interplay between architecture, optimization dynamics, and initialization. The proposed Adam-LR optimizer could also be a useful tool for practitioners.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates clear novelty in identifying the role of embeddings in grokking. The identification of the *mechanisms* causing embedding layers to induce grokking, and the successful attempt to mitigate these via Adam-LR justifies a high score. The experiments are well-designed, the analysis is thorough, and the findings are potentially impactful. The limitations regarding experimental scope (simple algorithmic tasks, limited direct Transformer validation, lack of improved results), keep the score from being higher. A score above 7 reflects the positive impact this paper could potentially have on grokking and transformer model optimization.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Listen to the Context: Towards Faithful Large Language Models for Retrieval Augmented Generation on Climate Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15633v1)
- **Authors**: David Thulke, Jakob Kemmler, Christian Dugast, Hermann Ney
- **Abstract**: Large language models that use retrieval augmented generation have the potential to unlock valuable knowledge for researchers, policymakers, and the public by making long and technical climate-related documents more accessible. While this approach can help alleviate factual hallucinations by relying on retrieved passages as additional context, its effectiveness depends on whether the model's output remains faithful to these passages. To address this, we explore the automatic assessment of faithfulness of different models in this setting. We then focus on ClimateGPT, a large language model specialised in climate science, to examine which factors in its instruction fine-tuning impact the model's faithfulness. By excluding unfaithful subsets of the model's training data, we develop ClimateGPT Faithful+, which achieves an improvement in faithfulness from 30% to 57% in supported atomic claims according to our automatic metric.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the crucial problem of ensuring faithfulness in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) for climate-related questions. It focuses on automatic assessment of faithfulness and analyzes how instruction fine-tuning (IFT) impacts the faithfulness of ClimateGPT, a specialized LLM for climate science. By strategically excluding less faithful subsets of IFT data, the authors develop ClimateGPT Faithful+, significantly improving faithfulness from 30% to 57% based on an automated metric. The study uses a combination of existing automated evaluation techniques (RAGAS, FActScore, VeriScore) with modifications tailored to faithfulness assessment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Relevant Problem:** Addresses a critical issue in applying LLMs to climate science and other sensitive domains: factual hallucinations and the erosion of trust due to unfaithful generation.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** Employs a well-defined methodology using a combination of claim decomposition, evidence retrieval, and verification, leveraging established tools like RAGAS but with a focus on faithfulness rather than overall factuality.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study on the IFT data is a key strength, allowing for a targeted understanding of which types of training data negatively impact faithfulness. This insight is used to develop a better model.
    *   **Demonstrated Improvement:** Quantifiable improvement in faithfulness is achieved, with ClimateGPT Faithful+ showing a marked increase in the percentage of supported claims.
    *   **Validation:** Validation on an alternative RAG dataset from Climate Policy Radar and with a second faithfulness metric (LettuceDetect) bolsters the claims of improvement.
    *   **Reproducible Evaluation Setup:** They thoroughly detail implementation and evaluation procedures.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Automated Metrics:** Heavily relies on automated evaluation metrics. While the authors acknowledge the limitations of RAGAS and similar methods, a more robust human evaluation would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Specific Focus on ClimateGPT:** While valuable, the findings may be specific to the ClimateGPT model and dataset. It's unclear how well the insights and the resulting model would generalize to other domains and LLMs.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Mitigation Strategies:** Besides data ablation, the paper does not explore other techniques to improve faithfulness, such as modifying the RAG prompt, incorporating faithfulness objectives into training, or exploring different retrieval strategies. Future work could benefit by using methods that enrich unfaithful data with supporting passages to improve model behavior.
    *   **Climate-FEVER verification**: While the authors applied the metrics on the Climate-FEVER dataset, results are very low compared to similar methods, indicating the potential for improved validation techniques.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   **Targeted Faithfulness Evaluation:** The specific focus on evaluating and improving faithfulness in climate-related RAG systems is a valuable contribution. This distinguishes it from general factuality assessment.
    *   **IFT Data Analysis:** The analysis of IFT data and its impact on faithfulness is a novel and important finding that can inform the training of specialized LLMs. The IFT curation techniques are highly significant.
    *   **Practical Improvement:** Creating ClimateGPT Faithful+ demonstrates the potential to build more trustworthy and reliable climate-focused LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a clear, focused, and methodologically sound contribution to improving the reliability of LLMs in the climate domain. While the heavy reliance on automated metrics is a limitation, the insights gained from the IFT data analysis and the demonstrable improvement in faithfulness warrant a positive assessment. This work provides useful guidelines and a strong basis for future research on building more trustworthy LLMs for specialized domains.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Feature Extraction and Steering for Enhanced Chain-of-Thought Reasoning in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15634v1)
- **Authors**: Zihao Li, Xu Wang, Yuzhe Yang, Ziyu Yao, Haoyi Xiong, Mengnan Du
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) demonstrate the ability to solve reasoning and mathematical problems using the Chain-of-Thought (CoT) technique. Expanding CoT length, as seen in models such as DeepSeek-R1, significantly enhances this reasoning for complex problems, but requires costly and high-quality long CoT data and fine-tuning. This work, inspired by the deep thinking paradigm of DeepSeek-R1, utilizes a steering technique to enhance the reasoning ability of an LLM without external datasets. Our method first employs Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to extract interpretable features from vanilla CoT. These features are then used to steer the LLM's internal states during generation. Recognizing that many LLMs do not have corresponding pre-trained SAEs, we further introduce a novel SAE-free steering algorithm, which directly computes steering directions from the residual activations of an LLM, obviating the need for an explicit SAE. Experimental results demonstrate that both our SAE-based and subsequent SAE-free steering algorithms significantly enhance the reasoning capabilities of LLMs.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel framework to enhance chain-of-thought reasoning in large language models (LLMs) by steering the models' internal activations. The core idea is to extract task-relevant reasoning features from readily available vanilla CoT data, bypassing the need for expensive long CoT datasets or model retraining. Two approaches are presented: 1) SAE-based steering, which utilizes Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to extract interpretable features from vanilla CoT activations, and introduces "VS Decomposition" to separate verbal and symbolic reasoning processes, and 2) an SAE-free steering algorithm that directly computes steering directions from the residual activations of an LLM, removing the SAE dependency. The paper demonstrates that both techniques can significantly improve the reasoning capabilities of LLMs across several mathematical reasoning benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the SAE-free steering method and the "VS Decomposition" for feature extraction. Steering LLMs with extracted features is not entirely new, but the approach of deriving steering directions directly from residual activations without relying on pre-trained SAEs and high-quality or contrastive data is a significant contribution. The "VS Decomposition" helps filter out non-informative noise and improve the relevance of extracted features, offering a potential advancement in feature engineering for LLM steering. The combination of these two techniques is a notable aspect of this research.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its ability to enhance LLM reasoning using readily available resources. This addresses a key challenge in the field, which is the high cost and difficulty of acquiring high-quality long CoT datasets.  By circumventing this requirement, the paper makes CoT steering techniques more accessible and applicable to a wider range of LLMs. The improvements in mathematical reasoning performance demonstrated on various benchmarks suggest a real potential for practical impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Technique:** The SAE-free steering method is a novel contribution, potentially broadening the applicability of LLM steering.
*   **Resource Efficiency:** The method relies on readily available vanilla CoT data, reducing the dependency on expensive and scarce resources.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper presents experimental results on multiple benchmark datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of both proposed techniques.
*   **Interpretability:** The discussion around "VS Decomposition" and feature selection provides insights into the specific reasoning components being amplified.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is primarily focused on mathematical reasoning. It is unclear how well these techniques generalize to other types of reasoning tasks (e.g., logical reasoning, common-sense reasoning).
*   **Model Family Restriction:** The experiments primarily use the Llama family of models, which could limit the generalizability of the results. While Gemma models are also experimented with, a broader range of architectures would strengthen the claims.
*   **Reliance on GPT-4.1 for CoT Generation:** Using GPT-4.1 to generate the verbal and symbolic sub-processes introduces a dependency on external APIs and potential biases of GPT-4.1 itself.
*   **Some implementation details are light**: Derivation of Equation 7 is not explained in detail.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents significant novel contributions, limitations in the scope of evaluation and reliance on specific models and APIs warrant caution. While the idea has merit, more investigation is required for broader applicability.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FragFake: A Dataset for Fine-Grained Detection of Edited Images with Vision Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15644v1)
- **Authors**: Zhen Sun, Ziyi Zhang, Zeren Luo, Zeyang Sha, Tianshuo Cong, Zheng Li, Shiwen Cui, Weiqiang Wang, Jiaheng Wei, Xinlei He, Qi Li, Qian Wang
- **Abstract**: Fine-grained edited image detection of localized edits in images is crucial for assessing content authenticity, especially given that modern diffusion models and image editing methods can produce highly realistic manipulations. However, this domain faces three challenges: (1) Binary classifiers yield only a global real-or-fake label without providing localization; (2) Traditional computer vision methods often rely on costly pixel-level annotations; and (3) No large-scale, high-quality dataset exists for modern image-editing detection techniques. To address these gaps, we develop an automated data-generation pipeline to create FragFake, the first dedicated benchmark dataset for edited image detection, which includes high-quality images from diverse editing models and a wide variety of edited objects. Based on FragFake, we utilize Vision Language Models (VLMs) for the first time in the task of edited image classification and edited region localization. Experimental results show that fine-tuned VLMs achieve higher average Object Precision across all datasets, significantly outperforming pretrained models. We further conduct ablation and transferability analyses to evaluate the detectors across various configurations and editing scenarios. To the best of our knowledge, this work is the first to reformulate localized image edit detection as a vision-language understanding task, establishing a new paradigm for the field. We anticipate that this work will establish a solid foundation to facilitate and inspire subsequent research endeavors in the domain of multimodal content authenticity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FragFake, a new dataset and methodology for detecting localized edits in images, addressing the challenges of current binary classification methods and the lack of large-scale, high-quality datasets for modern image editing detection. FragFake is constructed using an automated pipeline involving diverse image editing models (open-source and commercial), text-based editing instructions generated by GPT-4o, and covers both object addition and replacement. The authors leverage Vision Language Models (VLMs) for both classification and localization of edits, fine-tuning several popular VLMs (LLaVA, Qwen, Gemma). The paper benchmarks VLM performance, conducts ablation studies on training parameters (LoRA rank, data balancing), and assesses the transferability of trained detectors across different editing models and tasks. The results demonstrate that fine-tuned VLMs, particularly Qwen2.5-VL, significantly outperform pre-trained models and exhibit strong performance in both classifying edited images and localizing the edited regions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper presents a novel approach by reformulating the problem of localized image edit detection as a vision-language understanding task. This contrasts with traditional computer vision methods that rely heavily on pixel-level annotations. The automated data generation pipeline is also a significant contribution. While individual components (using VLMs, synthetic data generation) are not entirely new, their combination for this specific task and the scale of the dataset are innovative.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need in the era of AI-generated content and image manipulation. The ability to detect subtle, localized edits is crucial for maintaining content authenticity and combating misinformation. The introduction of the FragFake dataset and the benchmarking of VLMs provide a valuable resource for future research in this area. The demonstration that VLMs can be effectively fine-tuned for this task opens up new avenues for developing more robust and scalable detection methods. The transferability analysis sheds light on the generalizability of detectors across different editing techniques, a crucial consideration for real-world deployment. The insights gained from the ablation studies regarding data balancing and LoRA rank are useful for guiding future research in this domain.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of localized image edit detection and the limitations of existing methods.
    * **Automated Dataset Generation:** The automated pipeline allows for scalable and extensible data generation, addressing the scarcity of suitable datasets.
    * **VLM-Based Approach:** The use of VLMs leverages their strong visual understanding capabilities and reduces the need for costly pixel-level annotations.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes extensive experiments, ablation studies, and transferability analyses to thoroughly evaluate the proposed methodology.
    * **Open Source:** The open-sourcing of the code and dataset facilitates reproducibility and promotes further research.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Editing Types:** The dataset only covers object addition and replacement. Expanding to include other types of edits (e.g., style transfer, background changes, manipulation of facial expressions) would enhance the dataset's completeness.
    * **Limited Diversity of Editing Methods:** While four editing models are used, increasing the number and diversity of models would make the dataset more representative of real-world editing scenarios.
    * **Lack of Automated Filtering on Training Data:** The lack of automated filtering on training data could lead to some level of noise in the training data.
    * **Computational Cost:**  While LoRA is employed, the finetuning of VLMs can still be computationally expensive. The resources required may limit accessibility for some researchers.
    * **Potential Negative Impacts:** It is not clear how the creators will prevent bad actors from leveraging the methods described in the paper to make undetectable image edits.
